# Defects register — gaps, bugs, and defects to fix in the rebuild

> **Living document.** Ian, 2026-08-23: *"these surfacing gaps, bugs and defects — document
> them so we can fix as we build this project."*
>
> ### A defect here is never an argument against the feature
> Bible §1.2b — every feature in a reference repo exists because the business needed it and
> uses it daily. **A flawed implementation is evidence of a real need, not a proposal to drop.**
> Log the defect, harvest the intent, build it correctly here. If a feature looks pointless,
> **ask Ian** — something in daily operations probably explains it.
>
> ### ⛔ Nothing here is fixed at the source
> All four reference repos — `ERP-rebuild`, `POS`, `CUSTOMER`, `Shareat Website` — are
> **read-only** (bible §2 rule 1, §13 row 80). Every entry below is fixed **in the rebuild**.
> A defect is logged here, its business rule is harvested, and the correct behaviour is built
> into Shareat System. We never patch the reference.

## How to use this

- **Add a row the moment a defect is found.** Cite `file:line`. An unlogged finding is a lost one.
- ⭐ **Including one you found in passing** (bible §13 row 164). If you tripped over it while
  reading v1 for something else entirely, it still gets a row before you move on. *"It wasn't my
  task"* is not a reason to skip it — you are the only person who will ever have that context.
- ⛔ **Is it actually a defect?** A **defect** is v1 doing something *wrong*. Something v1 does
  that **no phase of the rebuild owns** is a **gap**, and it belongs in
  [`CAPABILITY-GAPS.md`](./CAPABILITY-GAPS.md) instead — this register's dispositions cannot
  express *"nobody has this"*, so a gap filed here is a gap buried here. The capability-gap pass
  is what caught the five ledger-posting modules January needed.
- **Verified means *I* read the code**, not that an agent reported it. Agent findings are
  `reported` until checked — three of four explorers on 2026-08-23 made at least one claim that
  did not survive verification.
- When a rebuild task closes a defect, mark it and name the task.

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Sev** | ⛔ correctness/money · ⚠️ structural risk · 🔸 quality/maintenance |
| **State** | `verified` (read the code) · `reported` (agent, unchecked) · `fixed` |
| **Disposition** | What the rebuild does about it |

---

## A. Money and correctness ⛔

| # | Sev | App | Defect | State | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-001** | ⛔ | POS | **Refund receipt numbers collide.** Refunds write `receiptSeq: 0` and `receiptNumber: RF-{parent}` with **no sequence suffix** — a second partial refund against the same parent mints an *identical* receipt number. `POS/src/components/cashier/firestore.ts:1484-1485` | `verified` | Refunds are first-class documents with their own sequence. Receipt number is UNIQUE per store by constraint, not convention |
| **DEF-002** | ⛔ | POS | **Two sale-completion paths write the same `receiptSeq` from non-comparable sequence spaces** — server-arbitrated counter online, per-device daily counter offline (`<store>-OL-<deviceTag>-<HHMM>-<NNN>`) | `verified` | Model honestly: composite `(device_id, local_seq)` for offline + a separate server sequence. Never one integer column pretending to be both. ADR-003 |
| **DEF-003** | ⛔ | POS | **Money stored as JS floats** (decimal pesos), not integer centavos — and **two duplicate rounding implementations**, `lib/money.ts` plus an inline copy in `lib/tax.ts` | `reported` | Already solved: `src/lib/money.ts` integer centavos, `numeric(14,2)` in DB. Bible §6. One implementation, no second copy |
| **DEF-004** | ⛔ | POS | **`receipt.source` is written but never read** by the analytics aggregation layer, so online sales silently fold into the `walkIn` bucket — which feeds ERP's `takeOutSales` | `verified` | `fulfilment` is a required column on the receipt table from day one. T-113 §6.7 |
| **DEF-005** | ⛔ | POS | **No take-out flag exists at all.** `sessionMode` has two values; walk-in à la carte and in-store take-out are the *same* value. Online orders live in a separate `onlineOrders` collection that *does* carry `fulfillment.type: pickup\|delivery`, never carried onto the receipt | `verified` | Harvest `fulfillment.type` onto the receipt as a **dimension**. The 4150/4200 account split was withdrawn 2026-08-23 — two food accounts only (unli, à la carte), so this is now about channel/fulfilment reporting, not revenue classification. T-113 §6.6 |
| **DEF-006** | ⛔ | ERP | **No nominal ledger.** Single-leg entries treated as "trivially balanced"; revenue and expense never post as journal legs; the income statement is a hand-maintained scan list | `verified` | **The reason this project exists.** Bible §16.0. Phase 1 |
| **DEF-065** | ⛔ | ERP-rebuild | **A split expense funded twice from the SAME sub-account silently loses one leg's audit trail.** `expense.builder.ts:19-135` derives each split's `legKey` from the sub-account it routes to — `cash:cashier`, `cash:office`, `cash:staff`, `online:{accountId}` — and **not** from the split's array index. `buildJournalEntryId(sourceType, sourceId, legKey)` (`journal-writer.ts:89`) turns that into a deterministic document id, so two splits with the same legKey resolve to the SAME journal entry doc, and `t.set(p.entryRef, entryData)` (`journal-writer.ts:168`) makes the second write a literal overwrite inside one transaction. One split's amount survives in the ledger; the other's does not, while the account balance — maintained separately by `FieldValue.increment` — may still reflect both. ⚠️ **This is reachable through a sanctioned feature, not an edge case.** `liquidation.ts:36-48` explicitly documents and supports *“a single receipt split across MULTIPLE cash requests”*, and every cash-request-funded split produces the same `cash:staff` legKey regardless of which request funded it. Two cashier-cash rows, or two online rows against one account, collide the same way. ⭐ **The same file already knows the fix.** Its reimbursable branch uses `reimbursable:{staffId}:{idx}` and `salary-advance.builder.ts:116` uses `${legSuffix}:${idx}` — both index-suffixed precisely to avoid this. The omission is builder-specific, not systemic, which is why it survived. It matters here more than anywhere else: expense payment is **4,396 legs, ~88% of v1's ledger activity**, and split funding is one of its named variants. | `reported` | v2 does not inherit the shape: `expense_payments` is a real child table with one row per split and its own `id`, and `postEntry` writes lines under a single entry rather than one document per leg, so two splits to the same account are two rows and cannot overwrite each other. ⚠️ **What v2 must NOT copy is the legKey idea itself** — any future per-leg deterministic key must include the split's identity, not just its destination account. **Worth an empirical check before cutover:** how many v1 expenses carry two splits resolving to one sub-account? Those entries' journal legs are already wrong in the live books, and `reconcileAccounts()` would have logged the drift only if someone ran it. |
| **DEF-069** | ⚠️ | **Shareat System** | ⭐ **A hand-written migration whose journal `when` is EARLIER than the previous entry's is silently skipped — and `db:migrate` still prints “Migrations applied”.** `packages/db/migrations/meta/_journal.json`'s timestamps are **synthetic, exactly 86,400,000 ms apart**, and drizzle applies entries by `when > last applied`. Journalling a new migration with `Date.now()` — the obvious thing to do — produces a value **behind** the synthetic series (0026 carried `1788319907084`, roughly a week ahead of real time), so the migration is treated as already applied. ⛔ **Nothing fails.** The command exits 0, prints the same success line, and records nothing in `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations`. Caught on 0027 only because the table was probed directly afterwards; a `\dt` or an assumption would have missed it, and the first failure would have surfaced much later as a missing-relation error against a schema file that looked perfectly correct. | `fixed` | ⭐ **Journal a new migration as `previous.when + 86400000`, never `Date.now()`** — the series is a monotonic ordinal, not a clock. ⚠️ **And verify the migration landed by querying `information_schema`, never by trusting the exit code**, exactly as “Deploy complete!” is not readiness for a Firestore index. Both databases are checked here (`shareat_dev` and `shareat_test`), because a migration that reaches only one produces integration tests that pass against a schema the app does not have. |
| **DEF-070** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild (its OWN bucket — **not** shared) · ⏸️ **ACCEPTED, deferred by Ian 2026-08-27** | ⭐ **ANY SIGNED-IN USER CAN READ EVERY FILE IN THE STORAGE BUCKET — every store, every ORG.** `storage.rules:34-36` is `match /{allPaths=**} { allow read: if request.auth != null; }`. There is no store check and no org check, so a franchise owner signed into their own org can read another org's receipt photos, purchase proofs, memo documents and onboarding files. ⚠️ **Found while specifying T-141, in the rules file's own neighbourhood.** The block immediately below it documents a *narrower* hole honestly — *“any signed-in user can write into any store's folder”* — and that admission is what ADR-002 §3 set out to close. The READ rule above it is broader, undocumented, and was not on anyone's list. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-27, and narrower than first registered.** I recorded this as sharing a bucket with POS and CUSTOMER. It does not: v1's ERP is alone in `studio-7300228488-a394e`, while POS and CUSTOMER share `shareat-hub-v2`. So the exposure is **across orgs inside the ERP**, and it never reached POS/CUSTOMER files or diners. The finding stands; its blast radius was overstated. ⭐ **And checking that turned up the more important fact**, now bible row 155: `shareat-hub-v2`'s bucket is `allow read;` — **no condition at all, world-readable** — which is correct for the menu photos it holds and is exactly why accounting documents must never go there. ⛔ **The asymmetry is the finding:** v2 spent T-120 putting franchise isolation into Postgres precisely because *“a franchise with a separate owner is already live (Padre Garcia)”* — RLS, `FORCE`, default-deny — while **the bytes describing those same books have no isolation at all**. A receipt photo is a business document; reading one is reading another owner's books by other means. | `verified` | ⭐ T-141 brokers reads as well as writes: no client touches the bucket, paths are derived server-side from the session, and reads go through a route that calls `authorize()` and mints a minutes-long signed URL. `storage.rules` then reduces to **deny-all**, which also removes the whole class of rules landmines this project has already recorded (OR-semantics, `firestore.get()` failing silently) — a rule you cannot get wrong beats one you have to get right. ⚠️ **Sequencing is the risk, not the code**: the bucket is shared with POS and CUSTOMER, which still write directly, and this project has already recorded a case of one repo's deploy overwriting another's ruleset. Tighten writes to the ERP service account first; deny-all last. ⏸️ **DEFERRED, and deliberately — Ian, 2026-08-27:** *“later when Shareat System is done we can touch the ruleset of the backend of v1, tighten or loosen what’s needed.”* ⭐ **This is a risk ACCEPTANCE with an end date, not a fix**, and it is the right call: v1 is live, its rules file is the one construct this project has repeatedly recorded as failing SILENTLY when edited wrong (`firestore.get()`, OR-semantics), and it cannot be verified from an Admin-SDK script because Admin bypasses rules. Editing it mid-cutover risks breaking a system being retired, to close a hole whose readers are franchise owners rather than the public. ⚠️ **What makes the acceptance safe is that v2 does not inherit it:** bible row 155 puts v2's documents in a NEW private bucket, so the exposure stops growing from today and ends when v1 does. ⛔ Revisit if v1's lifetime extends materially, or if a franchise relationship turns adversarial — the exposure is one owner reading another's supplier invoices, and that is a commercial secret, not just a privacy one. |
| **DEF-071** | ⛔ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **A MONTHLY OWNER DRAWING LIMIT IS SETTABLE, DISPLAYED ON SCREEN, AND NEVER ENFORCED.** `OwnerPlain.drawingLimitPeriod` is typed `'monthly' | 'total' | null` and the equity page writes either value (`app/accounting/equity/page.tsx:103`) and **renders it back to the user as a live constraint** — *“Drawing Limit (monthly) ₱50,000”* (`page.tsx:339-340`). But the only check in the whole repo is `equity.actions.ts:249`: `if (owner.drawingLimit && owner.drawingLimitPeriod === 'total')`. ⛔ **A `'monthly'` limit therefore falls through to no check at all** — the owner is shown a cap that does not exist, and a withdrawal of any size posts. ⚠️ **The failure mode is worse than an unenforced rule:** a displayed limit is a control people believe in and stop watching. `'total'` is enforced, which makes the omission look like an oversight in a code path someone was actively editing rather than a feature nobody finished. ⭐ Found while specifying T-127, by asking what enforces each field on the owner document. | `verified` | ⭐ T-127 enforces **both** periods, and enforces them **from the ledger** rather than from a stored total — the monthly window is a sum of `3200` for that owner within the calendar month, so the check cannot disagree with the drawings it is checking. ⚠️ The v1 check reads `owner.totalDrawings`, one of the three stored figures DEF-072 covers; a limit computed from a drifting total is a limit that fails in the direction of letting money out. |
| **DEF-075** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **`ARCustomerPlain.outstandingBalance` — A STORED BALANCE BESIDE THE LEDGER, FOR THE FIFTH TIME** (`types/index.ts:1574`). Loans (bible row 158), owner equity (DEF-072), cash requests, liabilities (DEF-074), and now customers. ⚠️ `ApSupplierPlain` carries the same column (`types/index.ts:420`), so it is really the fifth AND sixth. ⭐ **At this point the finding is not the column, it is the HABIT** — six modules, written at different times, each independently deciding the ledger needed a companion number. That is what makes “derived, never maintained” worth stating as a rule rather than fixing case by case. | `verified` | ⭐ T-129 derives it from `1210` by party from day one, so there is never a column for T-131 (AR) to have to remove later. ⭐ v1's `reconciledAsOfDate` anchor is PORTED and is not the same thing: it attests that a person verified a customer at zero on a date, which does not change when the ledger does — the same shape as T-127's drawings close, and the reason a later disagreement is visible. |
| **DEF-076** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | **`ap_open/{supplierId}/{expenseId}` is a hand-maintained Firestore projection that went stale in production.** `receiptId` was added to the projection write (`ap-projection.server.ts`) after the collection was already live, so every `ap_open` doc written before that change kept `receiptId: null` while the source `expense` doc had the correct value all along. The "Pay receipt" / "Credit Expense (Receipt)" flow (`ap-details-client.tsx`) groups and filters open items by `ap_open.receiptId`, so a receipt with 7 open items showed 1, with wrong totals — the projection, not the source document, was wrong. Same root cause as DEF-037 (no control account, so v1 has nothing that could have caught the drift) and the general class DEF-075 names: a second place holding a number the ledger/source already knows. | `reported` — prior-session field diagnostic (`[[project_ap_open_stale_receiptid]]`, 91 days old at time of citing); the specific incident is not re-verified against live v1 data this session, only the current write path (`ap-projection.server.ts`, read today, confirms `receiptId` is now written going forward — it does not confirm whether older docs still carry the stale value) | **Structurally closed by T-130's design, not by a fix in v1.** `docs/tasks/130-accounts-payable.md` Contract §4 builds no stored open-item projection at all — `openItems()` is a live query over `expenses` + `expense_payments` + `ap_payment_allocations`, so there is no upsert step to skip and nothing to go stale. If a receipt-grouping feature is ever needed, group by `expenses.supplier_invoice_id` read from the source rows directly, never from a cached copy. |
| **DEF-191** | ⚠️ | ERP | **v1 cannot write off a bad debt from any screen — `writeOffArTransactionAction` has no UI caller anywhere.** `ERP-rebuild/src/lib/actions/ar-credit-sale.actions.ts:453` implements a complete AR write-off (clamps to outstanding, posts the ledger delta with `onMissing: 'refuse'`), and grep across `src/app` and `src/components` finds nothing that calls it. So an uncollectible receivable stays in v1’s AR indefinitely: assets are overstated by exactly the amount nobody can declare dead, and the aging report ages it forever. Found 2026-09-06 reading v1’s AR module as the requirements source for T-226. ⭐ **Worth noting as evidence rather than as blame**: the “built, tested, no caller” shape this project keeps hitting is inherited, not invented here | `reported` | **Corrected in v2 by T-226 §7**, which is an *add* under the four verbs, not a duplicate — `writeOffCharge` (T-131) already exists, already refuses the charge’s own author, and gets its screen there. ⚠️ Do not read this as an argument against the feature (RULES §1.2b): the write-off action exists in v1 because somebody needed it, and the missing screen is why v1’s AR balance cannot be trusted. Cross-referenced from **DEF-190**. ✅ **Corrected 2026-09-06** — `/customers/[customerId]` ships the write-off, per charge, partial allowed, with the reason required and the second-pair-of-eyes refusal surfaced rather than pre-empted: the button still renders for the charge's own author, because hiding it would teach that write-off is unavailable when the truth is that it needs somebody else |

## B. Data integrity and architecture ⚠️

| # | Sev | App | Defect | State | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-007** | ⚠️ | POS + CUSTOMER | **Six collections written by BOTH apps** — `activeSessions`, `sessions`, `sessions/{id}/kitchentickets`, `rtKdsTickets/{loc}`, `customers/{phone}`, `loyaltyRedemptions`. Bible §4.2 rule 8 violated six times across a process boundary | `verified` | Disappears by construction under one backend. ADR-003 |
| **DEF-008** | ⚠️ | POS | **`merge:false` analytics rebuild** reconstructs kitchen and per-staff stats purely from surviving `kitchentickets` — purge the tickets and a rebuild silently zeroes them | `reported` | Derived data is a view or materialised view, never a hand-rebuilt document. Bible §4.2 rule 7 |
| **DEF-009** | ⚠️ | CUSTOMER | **Three separate identity schemes** — hashed device id (session participant), **phone number as document id** (loyalty, bcrypt), `d_`-prefixed device id (games) | `reported` | One `person` spine (bible §13 row 14). Phone is an attribute, never a primary key — it changes |
| **DEF-010** | ⚠️ | Website | **Three independently-synced sources of branch identity** — a content collection, a hardcoded `BRANCHES` array in the build script, and the live POS store doc | `reported` | One `stores` table. The whole class disappears |
| **DEF-011** | ⚠️ | Website | **`/menu` is baked at build time** from 3 hardcoded branch IDs; prices and copy go stale between deploys, and the build fails hard on a missing price | `reported` | Explicit publish step with a versioned snapshot, or live read. Decide in the Website phase |
| **DEF-072** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **OWNER EQUITY IS RECORDED IN THREE PLACES, AND ONE OF THEM STORES A SUBTRACTION.** (1) the journal — `equity__capital` legs; (2) the owner document — `totalCapital`, `totalDrawings`, and `netEquity`, whose own comment reads `// totalCapital - totalDrawings` (`types/index.ts:2958-2968`); (3) `EquityLedgerEntry`, carrying its own `balanceBefore` / `balanceAfter` (`equity.actions.ts:288-292`). ⛔ **This is the same shape as v1's loans** (bible row 158), found independently: four running totals, a second collection, and a ledger entry with its own running balance. ⚠️ **And a stored copy of a subtraction is the exact defect DEF-044 turned into a two-month outage** — a maintained register that nothing recomputes. ⭐ **The attribution itself is NOT the defect and should not be described as one:** v1 does put the owner on the entry, as `employeeId` (`equity-movement.builder.ts:57`) — a field named for something an owner is not, but present. The defect is the three registers. | `verified` | ⭐ T-127 keeps ONE register, the ledger. Every figure — contributed, drawn, net — is summed from `3100` and `3200` by party, so `net = contributed − drawn` is arithmetic on the ledger rather than a second definition of it. ⚠️ The owner row holds identity and policy (name, ownership %, drawing limit) and **no balances at all**. |
| **DEF-073** | ⚠️ | **Shareat System** | ⭐ **`npm run db:generate` IS UNUSABLE AND WOULD PROPOSE RE-CREATING 17 MIGRATIONS' WORTH OF SCHEMA.** The repo has **36 migrations and 12 snapshots** — `migrations/meta/` stops at `0019_snapshot.json`. Everything from 0020 on was hand-written, so drizzle's snapshot chain has been dead since 0019 and `db:generate` diffs the CURRENT schema against a seventeen-migration-old baseline. ⚠️ **It fails in the most misleading way available:** it opens an interactive prompt asking whether `useful_life_months` is a NEW column or a RENAME of `useful_life_years` — a question settled back in T-124, where the live database and the schema both already say `useful_life_months`. Answering either way writes a migration that re-does work already applied. ⛔ **The next person to add a table will reach for `db:generate` first**, because that is what the tool is for and nothing tells them otherwise — bible row 99 warns about hand-writing a `when` value but never says hand-writing is the NORM here. ⭐ Found while adding `owners` for T-127. | `closed 2026-09-10 → DEF-242 / ADR-012 / bible row 191` | ⚠️ **Not fixed here, and deliberately.** Regenerating the snapshot chain means reconciling 17 hand-written migrations against a schema that has moved a long way, mid-task, with no test that would catch a subtle mis-merge — exactly the kind of change that should not ride along with a feature. ⭐ **What IS done: the trap is now written down**, and hand-writing continues to be the working convention (0036 follows 0020–0035). ⛔ Whoever fixes it should do it as its own task, with `db:migrate` proven green from an empty database first and last — the only check that actually covers it. ⭐ **Closed by DEF-242 → ADR-012 (2026-09-10):** done exactly that way, and the answer is DOCUMENT — hand-authoring is now the recorded convention and `db:generate` fails closed with the reason |
| **DEF-074** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **A LIABILITY IS RECORDED IN THREE PLACES — THE FOURTH TIME THIS EXACT SHAPE HAS TURNED UP.** `LiabilityAccountPlain` carries `outstandingBalance`, `totalCharged` and `totalPaid` (`types/index.ts:2913-2928`); `LiabilityLedgerEntry` carries its own `balanceBefore` / `balanceAfter` (`types/index.ts:2933-2951`); and both sit beside the journal that already holds the same movements. ⚠️ **Identical to the loans (bible row 158), owner equity (DEF-072) and the cash requests** — found independently each time, which is what makes it a pattern in the codebase rather than one bad module. ⛔ `status: 'active' | 'settled'` is stored too, and “settled” just means the balance reached zero. ⭐ **And a second finding, which is an omission rather than a duplication: there is NO DUE DATE anywhere on a liability.** An obligation with no due date cannot be overdue, so the screen can show what is owed and never what is LATE — which is the question people open it to ask. | `verified` | ⭐ T-128 keeps one register. `charged`, `paid` and `outstanding` are summed from `2500` by party, `outstanding = charged − paid` is arithmetic on that balance, and `settled` is derived from it. ⭐ `due_on` is added, and overdue obligations surface above the register — a prompt, not a rule, the same shape as T-127's aged owner receivables. |
| **DEF-166** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | **`voidSalesDayAction`'S DOC-COMMENT CLAIMS A SNAPSHOT/MONTH-INDEX CASCADE ITS CODE NEVER RUNS.** The docstring (`ERP-rebuild/src/lib/actions/sales-day.actions.ts:164-165`) states that voiding a sales day "removes it from the month index, and enqueues ledger/snapshot recompute." The function body (`167-309`) does neither: no month-index write for this collection exists anywhere in the repo (grepped repo-wide for "month index" — zero hits outside the comment), and there is no call to the `cascadeSnapshots` chain the sibling Sales Report Adjustment flow explicitly runs for an equivalent-size restatement. So a void and an adjustment of the same magnitude leave downstream rollups in different states, and the comment asserts the safer of the two. Found in passing 2026-09-04 during the Phase 6 reporting discovery pass (`docs/discovery/26-reporting-dashboards-and-insights.md` §A.1, §A.4) | `verified` — code read directly | **Structural in v2, not a port.** A sales-day void is `reverseEntry` inside one transaction (bible §4.2 rule 9), and every downstream figure is a live query over `journal_lines` (phase-6 plan §2), so there is no snapshot to cascade to and no month index to forget. Recorded so the *intent* — a void must trigger the same re-derivation an equivalent adjustment does — is preserved rather than assumed. Closed by T-209/T-210's read model; no separate fix task. |

## C. Security and access ⚠️

| # | Sev | App | Defect | State | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-012** | ⛔ | POS/CUSTOMER | **CUSTOMER can overwrite POS's production ruleset.** CUSTOMER ships its own 85-line `firestore.rules` plus a `.firebaserc` alias `customerapp` → `shareat-hub-v2`, the same project POS uses. A deploy from CUSTOMER replaces POS's 800+ line ruleset. One-directional; POS has no reverse path | `verified` | One backend, one authorization module (bible §7 rule 1). No client-deployed rules govern business data |
| **DEF-013** | ⛔ | POS/CUSTOMER | **Flash-ad image upload fails closed in production.** CUSTOMER uploads to `flashAdImages/{storeId}/...` (`CUSTOMER/src/app/admin/flash-ads/page.tsx:234`); POS's `storage.rules` has no match block for that path and the catch-all is `allow write: if false` (`storage.rules:61-64`) | `verified` | File storage is an explicit, authorized server-side operation. Distinct from the known flash-ads *display* fix |
| **DEF-014** | ⚠️ | POS/CUSTOMER | **Checked-in rules diverge from deployed rules.** CUSTOMER's committed `firestore.rules` denies `activeSessions`, which its own live dashboard reads, and expects custom claims POS never sets — proof the file has never been deployed | `verified` | Rules live with the code that needs them and deploy from CI, not a laptop |
| **DEF-015** | ⚠️ | POS/CUSTOMER | **Local dev reaches production.** Both `.env.local` files reference a service-account key with the identical basename (`POS.json`) — either repo's local environment can write to live Firestore | `reported` | Environment isolation is enforced; production credentials never resolve from a dev `.env` |
| **DEF-016** | ⚠️ | Website | **Cross-project service-account credential.** The Website's Cloud Functions hold `HUB_SA_KEY`, a borrowed identity into POS's database (`shareat-hub-v2`); `MENU_BUILD_SA_KEY_PATH` is a second at build time | `reported` | Deleted outright — the seam it bridges no longer exists |
| **DEF-017** | ⚠️ | POS | **Connectivity judged by bare `navigator.onLine`** — no reachability probe, so a captive portal or dead uplink reads as "online" | `reported` | Real reachability probe against the API before declaring online |
| **DEF-018** | ⚠️ | POS | **The planned rules guardrail against a stale offline write clobbering an already-finalised receipt/session was never implemented** — confirmed absent from `firestore.rules` | `reported` | Posted records immutable by constraint (bible §4.2 rule 6). Replay is idempotent behind a UNIQUE key |
| **DEF-019** | 🔸 | CUSTOMER | **Admin PIN-registry delete is a raw client-side `deleteDoc`** | `reported` | Every mutation goes through an authorized server action. Bible §7 |
| **DEF-020** | 🔸 | Website | **Public writes into shared data** — reservations, online orders, a merge-only `customers/{phone}` upsert, and two different chat-thread models (bearer-id vs token-gated) | `reported` | Public input is validated server-side and never writes business state directly |
| **DEF-077** | ⚠️ | **Shareat System** | **RLS policies since migration 0036 use raw `current_setting('app.org_id', true)::uuid` instead of `app_current_org()`**, the project's own SQL function, which wraps the same call in `NULLIF(..., '')` before casting. ⚠️ **This IS reachable in production, on a pooled connection — corrected after an initial disposition wrongly called it theoretical.** The mechanism: `set_config('app.org_id', $1, true)` is `SET LOCAL` on a custom (placeholder) GUC. When that transaction commits, PostgreSQL resets the GUC to `''` (empty string), **not** to unset/NULL — a documented quirk of custom GUCs that only ever had a `LOCAL` assignment, and exactly why `app_current_org()`'s `NULLIF(..., '')` guard exists at all. `principal.ts`'s own customer branch is the reachable path: *"Customer: NO org is set, so every org-scoped policy evaluates against NULL"* — true on a **fresh** connection, false on a **pooled, reused** one that previously served a staff request: `app.org_id` reads back `''`, not NULL. Reproduced live with a `max: 1` pool (forcing reuse): a staff transaction sets a real `org_id` and commits; the next transaction on the same connection, applying a customer principal exactly as `applyPrincipal` does, reads `current_setting('app.org_id', true)` as `''`; every table still on the raw idiom throws `22P02` where `app_current_org()`'s tables cleanly deny with zero rows. Verified independently twice — once by this row's author (superuser connection, which bypasses RLS via `rolbypassrls=true` and initially produced a false "not reachable" conclusion), then corrected by a peer session's second pass, which is the version above, itself re-verified live before writing this row. **Still not a data leak** — a policy-evaluation error still returns zero rows to the client, it just does it as a 500 instead of a clean empty result — but it is a **live, reachable, customer-facing availability defect**: any customer-kind request whose connection was previously used for a staff request will 500 on any of the ~10 affected tables, unpredictably, depending on pool reuse history. Found by a peer session's audit of T-130, mid-wrap-up; the reachability correction is from that same peer's second pass | `verified` | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-28 — all of it, both databases.** Migration `0044` DROP/CREATEs the six remaining policies (`customers`, `price_lists`, `price_list_items`, `liabilities`, `owners`, `owner_drawing_closes`) onto `app_current_org()`; T-130's own four were already done in `0043`. ⚠️ **The count in this row's description was wrong — it is SIX, not ~10**, confirmed by querying `pg_policies` on both databases rather than grepping migration text. Both now read **44 of 44 policies on `app_current_org()`, zero raw**. ⛔ A new migration, never an edit: 0036-0042 are applied, and editing one would not re-run it — the source would look fixed while the live policies stayed broken. ⭐ **Two regression tests, enumerated from the live catalogue** (`packages/db/tests/integration/rls.test.ts`): one asserts no policy anywhere matches the raw idiom, so a table added later is covered without anyone remembering; the other proves a no-principal write is refused with `42501` (clean denial) and never `22P02`, on a deliberately `max: 1` pool that forces the connection reuse the bug needs. Both failed first, for the right reason. ⚠️ **Why the existing suite missed this for three tasks:** `rls.test.ts`'s invariant-11 test already exercised the exact scenario — sequential transactions on a pooled connection, then no principal — but asserted against `stores` alone. The invariant was proven for one hand-picked table instead of every table with a policy. That is the actual lesson, and it is why both new tests enumerate. |
| **DEF-078** | ⚠️ | **Shareat System** | **`npm run month-end` exists, is idempotent and is tested — and nothing on any host invokes it.** T-140 §4 deliberately chose a CLI over an HTTP route (POS's `gcloud` job output *echoes* `CRON_SECRET`, and an authenticated public surface buys nothing here), leaving the trigger to “the host's cron or a systemd timer”. That trigger has never been installed, and there is no deployment host yet, so the omission is **unowned rather than broken**. ⛔ **This is v1's exact failure mode, for the fourth time**: DEF-032 (nightly reconciliation), DEF-033 (three more routes) and DEF-044 (depreciation silently stopped for two months, ₱35,483.98/month unbooked) are all *code present, nothing triggering it*. ⭐ **What makes it worse here than in v1**: T-140's whole premise is that `scheduler_runs` distinguishes “it ran and there was nothing to do” from “it never ran” — a table that stays empty proves the second, but only if somebody looks. Found 2026-08-28 by tracing callers of `runMonthEnd`, after this project's own roadmap was edited to claim it “runs monthly since 27 Aug” — an overstatement written and corrected the same hour | `verified` | ✅ **(b) DONE 2026-08-28 — T-135 shipped it.** `periodicPostingsMissed` fires `depreciationNotBooked` / `amortisationNotBooked` exactly as specified below, compared against OPEN BALANCES, with a test proving a recorded no-op run silences it and an absent run does not. ⚠️ **(a) is still open and still belongs on the T-134 cutover checklist** — there is no host yet, so nothing fires the runner and the finding will be TRUE the moment a period completes. That is the intended behaviour: it is now loud instead of silent. Original disposition: **Belongs on the T-134 cutover checklist, not in application code.** Two things close it: (a) install the timer when the host exists, and (b) an integrity finding (T-135) that fires when an org has an open prepaid balance or a depreciable asset and **no `scheduler_runs` row for the last completed period** — DEF-044's lesson is that the check must compare against open balances, never against last month's activity, or a schedule that simply finished looks identical to one that died. ⚠️ Until (a) is done, depreciation and amortisation post only when a human runs the command. |
| **DEF-079** | ⛔ | **Shareat System** | **`scripts/cutover-purge.ts` cannot run. It `DELETE`s the purge-class tables, and `journal_entries` carries a BEFORE DELETE trigger that raises on any posted row** — *"posted journal entry % cannot be deleted"* (`0003_journal_entries_and_lines.sql`, bible invariant 5). Every real ledger is posted rows, so the purge fails on the first one and the whole transaction rolls back. ⭐ **Proved, not reasoned**: deleting a posted entry in `shareat_test` inside a rolled-back transaction returned the trigger's own message. ⚠️ Nothing caught this because the purge script has no test that runs it against POSTED data — T-119's own tests exercise the classification and the ordering, which are both correct; it is the delete itself that cannot execute. Found 2026-08-29 while building T-134's C2 button, which is exactly the failure the November rehearsal exists to surface | `verified` | ✅ **FIXED same day: `TRUNCATE`, not `DELETE`.** TRUNCATE does not fire row-level DELETE triggers — that is defined behaviour, not a loophole — so the invariant stays fully armed for every ordinary write path while the one operation allowed to empty the ledger can do so. ⭐ Chosen over the alternatives deliberately: a GUC exemption inside the trigger would be *a flag a code path can forget to unset*, and `session_replication_role = 'replica'` disables FK enforcement too, which is far more than this needs. ⚠️ Safe because **every table referencing the ledger is itself purge-class** (verified against `pg_constraint`: ap_payments, ar_charges, ar_collections, ar_write_offs, channel_settlements, expenses, money_transfers, sales_days, trade_orders — none KEEP), so no kept row can be orphaned. TRUNCATE is transactional in PostgreSQL, so the script's one-transaction-with-post-conditions shape is unchanged. ⚠️ date_scoped and keep_shell tables still use DELETE/UPDATE, because those are partial by definition |

| **DEF-093** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **THE PRODUCTION "COOK" PATH NEVER CONVERTS UNITS — A 500 g RECIPE LINE DEDUCTS 500 KILOS.** `src/lib/inventory/recipe-units.ts` exists precisely to stop this, and says so: *"the usage post never converts quantities — it only relabels the unit from the product master. So if a recipe says '500 g' and the item is tracked in kg, an unconverted post deducts 500 KILOS. Every caller must either convert here or block the row; silently passing the number through is the one outcome that must be impossible."* ⛔ **Its own caller does exactly that.** `production-recipe.actions.ts` invokes its private `resolveUnitConversion(line.unit, line.unit)` — **an identity call**, source unit against source unit, which can never convert and never fails. `recipe-units.ts:11-16` names the call shape and says *"do not replicate that call shape; pass the ITEM's unit as the target"*, and flags repointing production at it as *"a separate follow-up"* that was never done. ⚠️ So the guard is written, correct, and **wired to nothing** on the cook path. The magnitude is 1000×, silent, and lands in the stock ledger as a real deduction. | `reported` — read at source 2026-08-29 (`recipe-units.ts:1-17,49-57`, `production-recipe.actions.ts:48-60`). ⚠️ **Not confirmed against live data**: whether any live recipe line actually states a unit different from its item's base unit is unverified, and if none does the identity call has never bitten. Stated as reported per §13 row 164 — verification is not required to log | ⭐ **Structurally closed by T-176**, which specifies ONE conversion module (`packages/domain/src/production/unit-conversion.ts`) with a test asserting there is no second copy, and a hard block — never a pass-through — when a pairing is not convertible. ⚠️ The deeper fix is that v2's quantities are exact decimals in a known base unit, so "relabel without converting" is not expressible: a recipe line carries its own unit and must resolve against the item's before it can produce a movement at all |

Found while researching T-191 (13th-month pay, confirmed nowhere in v1 per bible row 90) — the manual's claim is the one piece of evidence that looked like it might contradict "neither exists in the legacy system," and it does not hold up | `reported` — found 2026-09-01 while assessing T-191, not independently verified beyond the repo-wide grep | Same shape as DEF-024 (stale doc) and DEF-034 (a capability-inventory agent trusting a doc over live state) — a generated manual is not evidence of a feature. Nothing to port; T-191 in Shareat System is the real thing this text gestures at |

## E. Found in Shareat System itself

| # | Sev | Defect | State | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DEF-031** | ⚠️ | **Posted-entry immutability has a hole on INSERT.** The trigger blocks UPDATE and DELETE on a posted entry, per T-103's contract — but a client writing raw SQL could still INSERT a *new balanced pair* of lines onto an already-committed posted entry, changing its content without tripping the balance rule. Found and reported by the T-103 implementer, which first over-implemented a blanket INSERT block, discovered it made legitimately building a posted entry inside one transaction impossible, and reverted rather than shipping a broken constraint | **`fixed`** | **Closed WITHOUT `xmin`/`txid_current()`** — T-105's actual fix is draft-first ordering, not transaction-id comparison (that escape hatch turned out to be unnecessary, contrary to this row's original disposition). `postEntry` (`packages/domain/src/ledger/post-entry.ts`) always inserts the entry as `status='draft'`, inserts every line while it is still draft, then flips it to `posted` as the LAST statement. Migration `0004_journal_lines_posted_immutable_insert.sql` replaces `journal_lines_posted_immutable` (now `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE`) so a line can never be INSERTed onto an entry whose status is not `draft` — closing the hole for every path, not just `postEntry`'s. Closing test: `packages/domain/tests/integration/post-entry.test.ts`, invariant 14 ("DEF-031 regression: raw SQL cannot INSERT a line onto an already-posted entry" — asserts a balanced pair is rejected even from a brand-new, later transaction). **Caveat found while closing this**: the new trigger also rejects `packages/db/tests/integration/journal.test.ts`'s pattern of constructing a posted-entry fixture by inserting `journal_entries` with `status='posted'` directly and then inserting its lines (non-draft-first) — 4 pre-existing tests in that file now fail for that reason. See the T-105 task report |
| **DEF-035** | ⛔ | **The seeded chart of accounts is missing 17 accounts that COA-ALIGNMENT §4 requires** — `5110` `5120` `5130` `5140` `5150` `5160` `5170` (the COGS breakdown by category) and `6160` `6510` `6620` `6630` `6650` `6850` `6860` `6870` `6880` `6890` (OPEX detail). **My T-102 spec is the cause:** it said *"take the complete list from COA-ALIGNMENT §2"*, but §2 gives account **groups** while **§4** gives the finer category→account mapping and introduces these seventeen. The implementer seeded exactly what I asked for | **`fixed`** | **Blocks T-113** — the expense-category mapping routes to accounts that do not exist. Fixed by T-102b: migration `0006_coa_expense_category_accounts.sql` adds the 17 leaf accounts (parented `5000`/`6000` per code range, no `legacy_code`, `is_system=true`, `status='active'`), and `packages/db/seed/chart-of-accounts.ts`'s `CHART_OF_ACCOUNTS` grows from 75 to 92 rows to match. `5170` COGS — commissary transfers carries the inter-store/double-count elimination warning from §4.1 as a source comment (no `memo` column exists on `accounts`; schema was out of scope). Closing test: `packages/db/tests/integration/chart-of-accounts.test.ts`, describe block **"T-102b invariant 7 — every account code named anywhere in COA-ALIGNMENT §4 exists in the chart"** — it parses §4's own markdown table rows for bold `**NNNN**` codes (not a hand-maintained list) and asserts each exists in the freshly seeded chart; confirmed failing (listing exactly these 17 missing codes) before the migration/seed change, and passing after. The other 6 T-102b invariants (type/normal_balance, parenting, no legacy_code, active/is_system, idempotent re-seed, 92-account total) are the same file's "T-102b invariant 1" through "6" blocks. Lesson recorded: a spec that points at one section of a two-level source produces a half-complete result, and the half that is missing is invisible until something tries to use it |
| **DEF-036** | ⛔ | **A `reversed` entry's lines were mutable by raw SQL.** Migration `0004`'s immutability branch checked `entry_status = 'posted'` only. Nothing had ever produced a `'reversed'` entry, so the hole was **dormant** — T-106 would have created the first one, leaving every reversed entry's lines editable and directly breaking invariant 6 (*nothing deleted, no amount changed on the original*). Found by the T-106 implementer **while implementing something else**, and outside its own contract | **`fixed`** | Migration `0005` checks `entry_status IN ('posted','reversed')`. **The lesson: a guard written against the states that exist today silently stops guarding when a new state is introduced.** Any status added to `journal_entries` from here must be checked against every trigger that switches on status |
| **DEF-037** | ⛔ | **Invariant 6 is currently unsatisfiable: there is no subledger.** `journal_lines` carries `account_id`, `store_id`, `channel_id` — **no counterparty**. Invariant 6 requires *"AR detail total = AR control balance"* — there is no detail to total. Undetected because Phase 1 has not yet posted an AP or AR line. Needs a follow-up task to populate `party_type`/`party_id` in the ETL adapter |

This is the specific reason the T-114b audit exists: to give per-party AP/AR totals that can be checked against the sheet rather than trusted  **✅ IMPACT RESOLVED 2026-08-24 — Ian: “AR/AP are zero for that period.”** There were no open payables or receivables at 30 April 2026, so there was nothing to seed and nothing is missing. **The finding itself stands unchanged** — v1 contains no AP/AR seeding entry, and anyone reading those books later would have no way to tell “never seeded” from “seeded as zero”. But the consequence for the cutover is **nil**: Ian's per-party lump sums **can** be taken from v1 alone after all, and no figure needs adding from the Jan–Apr sheet. | **DEF-044** | ⚠️ | **Depreciation and amortization stopped posting after 30 June 2026 — two months and counting.** `depreciation` (543 legs) and `amortization` (57 legs) both have `max(businessDate) = 2026-06-30`, against a capture taken 2026-08-24. Schedules run back to 2026-01-31, so these were being posted regularly and then stopped | `reported` | **July and August depreciation/amortization are unbooked**, so v1 currently overstates net income and overstates net fixed assets. Likely a scheduler that stopped — consistent with the standing open question *“are ERP's 9 cron routes actually live-scheduled?”* Needs checking in v1 **before the December close**, since every month it stays broken is another month of wrong figures feeding the 2026 close and therefore the opening balance  **✅ CONFIRMED by Ian 2026-08-24: “yes not running, need to book depreciation.”** Not a data artefact — the scheduler is genuinely stopped. **Action sits with Ian in v1**, and it compounds: every month unbooked is another month of overstated net income and overstated net fixed assets feeding the 2026 close and therefore the 1 January opening balance.  **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-25.** Ian ran depreciation: **80 legs each for 2026-07-31 and 2026-08-31, ₱35,483.98 per month**, verified by re-capture — `depreciation`'s latest `businessDate` moved from 2026-06-30 to 2026-08-31. **`amortization` stopping at 2026-06-30 is NOT a defect** — Ian: *“nothing to amortize.”* The prepaid schedules simply completed; 57 legs ran and finished. **Worth recording because the data looks identical either way**: a stopped scheduler and a finished schedule both show as “no legs since June”, and only Ian could tell them apart. A monitor that flags *“no amortization this month”* would have cried wolf here — so v2's freshness check must compare against **open prepaid balances**, not against last month's activity. |
| **DEF-045** | 🔸 | **`cash_legacy_import` legs run from 2026-05-01 to 2026-08-19, not just the cutover date.** A legacy import is by definition a one-time seeding event; 11 of 12 legs are dated at the cutover and at least one lands in August | `reported` | Probably a cash account added or corrected long after the seeding, using the legacy-import type as a convenience. Harmless in itself, but it means **`*_legacy_import` cannot be treated as “the opening balance entry” when reading v1** — filtering by sourceType alone would sweep in a mid-year adjustment. T-114b must date-bound it |
| **DEF-041** | ⚠️ | **The ETL landing table was mutable — raw SQL could rewrite or delete a landed source document.** `etl_source_documents` is the reproducibility anchor of the whole migration: the “land before you convert” design (T-112) rests on the September bytes being the December bytes, so a difference in output is necessarily a difference in *our* code. **The cause is my own T-112 spec**, whose invariant 5 read *“the framework never UPDATEs or DELETEs a landed row”* — a statement about our code's behaviour rather than a constraint. The implementer satisfied it exactly, proving it by grepping `repo/etl.ts` for `.update(etlSourceDocuments)`. Verified empirically 2026-08-23: a plain `UPDATE` rewrote a landed payload from 100 to 999999 and a plain `DELETE` removed the row, both silently. **The damage would be invisible** — reconciliation still balances, every count still ties, and the audit report quietly describes data that no longer exists | **`fixed`** | Migration `0009_etl_landing_immutable.sql` — a `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` trigger, same treatment as DEF-031 for the same reason. **INSERT stays open**: landing is append-only, not frozen, so a changed source document re-lands as a new row beside the old one (invariant 4), and a test asserts that was not collateral damage of the fix. **No exemption of any kind** — unlike 0005's reversal GUC, no legitimate operation needs to rewrite history here. Closing tests: `packages/etl/tests/integration/etl-run.test.ts`, describe **“DEF-041 — the landing table is immutable in the database”** (4 tests: UPDATE blocked with the original bytes intact, DELETE blocked, re-landing still permitted, and the error names the `doc_path` so an operator can act on it). **Lesson: an invariant phrased as “our code does not do X” is a convention. Phrase it as what the DATABASE refuses, or it is not an invariant** |
| **DEF-042** | ⚠️ | **Quarantine reason CODES do not exist yet — `convert.ts` emits only free-text prose.** T-112's contract requires an aggregable `reason_code` (*“880 `unmapped_expense_category`, 407 `unbalanced_source_group`”*) because the audit report's entire value is the aggregate; a log of 1,412 distinct English sentences is not actionable. But `convertSpecimen` returns a single free-text `reason` and no code. **Found and reported by the T-112 implementer, which correctly refused to derive a code by pattern-matching that prose** (bible §11 rule 5 / T-113 §3 rule 1 — never guess a mapping) and instead made `reasonCode` an explicit required input on the framework's `Converter` seam | `verified` | **Blocks the T-114 audit report from being useful, not from running.** Fix in T-114: extend `convert.ts` to emit a `reasonCode` alongside its existing free-text `reason` — an enumerated union, not a derived string — and pass it through the seam T-112 already provides. Do **not** classify by regex over the prose; that reintroduces exactly the guessing both rules forbid |
| **DEF-046** | ⛔ | **`convert.ts` converted v1's floats with `money()`, which THROWS on sub-centavo precision — so the first real conversion run CRASHED.** Twelve call sites (`money(doc.totalSales)`, `money(payment.amount)`, …) passed raw v1 payload floats straight into the strict constructor. Real data contains such values; the first full audit hit `648.6479999999999` and aborted the entire run. **It passed every corpus specimen** because those are hand-authored with clean 2dp figures | **`fixed`** | A `legacyMoney()` helper wrapping `moneyFromLegacyFloat` — the ETL-only converter built for exactly this, which rounds half away from zero **matching v1's own `roundMoney()`** so a migrated figure reproduces v1's, and reports rather than throws. All 12 sites converted. **Lesson: a fixture corpus authored by hand cannot surface a data-shape defect, because the author unconsciously writes well-formed values.** Only the real capture could find this — which is an argument for capturing early, not for writing more fixtures |
| **DEF-047** | ⚠️ | **`moneyFromLegacyFloat`'s precision check produced false positives on large amounts**, because its tolerance was ABSOLUTE (`1e-9`) while float representation error scales with magnitude. `333992.46` — exact to the centavo — computes `value * 100` as `33399245.999999996`, an error of 3.7e-9, and was reported as precision loss. `money()` in the same file already used `1e-6` | **`fixed`** | Tolerance aligned to `1e-6`, matching `money()`. Verified against real values: `333992.46` now passes; the genuine sub-centavo cases `263.88888888888886` and `648.648` still flag. **The cost of the bug was not the wrong number — it was that ~8 false findings sat in the same list as the real ones, and a finding class you cannot trust is a finding class nobody reads** |
| **DEF-048** | 🔸 | **v1 stores ~460 journal legs with genuine sub-centavo amounts** — computed values written without rounding first. Depreciation is the clearest case: `263.88888888888886` (an asset cost divided by a term), stored raw rather than rounded to `263.89` | `reported` | Not a v1 bug so much as a v1 habit, and it is **why the ETL must round the way v1's own `roundMoney()` rounds** rather than any other way — half away from zero — or migrated figures will not reproduce v1's. v2 prevents the class entirely: `numeric(14,2)` cannot hold a third decimal, so the value is rounded once at the boundary and never drifts again |

That is this row's own recorded resolution — *“the owner belongs to the ACCOUNT — it should be derived, never ticked”* — arrived at from the opposite direction. ⚠️ `cashRequestId` survives as METADATA, no longer as routing: it records which request funded a float, while the float's own account decides the posting. Closing tests: `expense-routing.test.ts` (a staff float routes to 1120 because the account says so; the office safe to 1130; cash with no account is refused) and the mirror test, which now documents that v1 and v2 take different INPUTS — v1 keeps its flags because its data has them — while still agreeing on the output. |

Rename a channel to "Restaurant" and the match stops. **Three faults in one field: a substring fallback, a silent failure mode, and money as the thing lost** | `verified` | v2: the channel a pull lands on is **explicit and required**, with no substring fallback. Two differently-named AI master switches (`aiInsightsEnabled` vs `aiEnabled`) do the same job. **`taxType` is the dangerous one: POS computes the customer's receipt from its own `taxType`/`taxRatePct` while the ERP keeps its own, so if they ever disagree the receipt in the customer's hand and the books disagree about VAT — and nothing compares them** | `verified` | **Dissolves in v2 by construction: one `stores` row, and `posStoreId` disappears with it.** ⚠️ But *“pick one copy”* is the wrong general fix — POS genuinely needs `taxType` at the point of sale and the ERP genuinely needs it for the books. **The rule is ONE OWNER, PUBLISHED to whoever needs it** (the bible's Lane A mirror), never one copy: exactly one side is editable, the other reads. Contrast the cash handover (discovery 23 §4), which IS a computation and therefore does have one right home |
| **DEF-061** | ⛔ | **The ERP's sales pull returns a revenue figure that is GROSS or NET depending on whether a backfill has run — and nothing tells the caller which.** `POS/src/app/api/external/sales/route.ts:45-53` reads `sales.dineInSalesGross` with the comment *“Dine-in sales = gross (before discounts/charges)”*, then falls back — *“Fallback to net if gross not yet backfilled”* — to summing `packageSalesAmountByName` + `dineInAddonSalesAmount`, which is NET. `takeOutSales` is then derived as `max(0, totalGross − dineInSales)`, so **a net `dineInSales` silently inflates `takeOutSales` by the whole discount amount.** The two branches differ by every discount and charge in the day and are indistinguishable downstream | `verified` | ⛔ **This is not a naming problem, it is a correctness one**: the ERP cannot know whether the revenue it received is comparable with the tender it received. v2 must **state the basis on the figure** — a pull records whether it is gross or net, and a day whose basis is unknown blocks rather than being assumed either way. ⚠️ Also worth measuring before the cutover: **which branch is actually firing in production today**, because that determines whether existing v1 sales-day revenue is gross or net — and therefore whether any v1 figure we reconcile against is comparable with another |

The code's own comment states the consequence: *“if it's unset the fallback below is publicly known (it's in the source), so anyone could forge a `passcodeLookupHash`.”* It **deliberately does not fail closed** — that would break the clock terminal — so it logs an error instead. ⚠️ And rotating the secret **invalidates every stored hash**, so the safe fix is also the disruptive one, which is precisely why an unset secret tends to stay unset. Also in the same file: `matchLegacyPasscode` iterates **bcrypt over every candidate**, which is O(n) hashing per attempt and a timing surface | `verified` | **v2 fails closed at STARTUP, not at use** (T-138 §3.2): a missing or default signing secret stops the process, with a test. *A control whose degraded mode is a publicly-known key is not degraded, it is off* — and a loud log is not a control, because the person who would act on it is not reading the logs at 9pm on a Saturday. PIN comparison is constant-time and O(1) in candidates. ⭐ **One part is KEPT**: the hash is scoped to the store *“so the same passcode in different stores never correlates”* — good thinking that survives unchanged |

That narrows the exposure; it does not remove it. **Do this before the Oct–Dec pilot puts a terminal in front of a cashier** |

They read straight off `getDb()`, which sets no RLS principal, over a `DATABASE_URL` connecting as `postgres` — a superuser, so RLS never applies either. **The org is taken from the query string** (`sp.org`, validated only as "an active org that exists"), so `?org=<uuid>` selects whose books you read. **The page also enumerates every active org into its picker**, handing an anonymous visitor the exact ids it needs. Not a redirect to sign-in — the real report. ⚠️ **This survived the T-138 sweep and the 2026-08-26 daily review, both of which recorded auth as ✅ moved server-side.** It was missed because the capabilities exist and look enforced: `view_trial_balance` and `view_financial_reports` are in `CAPABILITY_MATRIX`, and `tests/unit/no-client-supplied-actor.test.ts` passes — but that guard only scans `'use server'`/`/api/`/`route.ts` files for *actor* field names, and these are pages reading an *org* id, so it cannot see them. **This is DEF-051 again in v2's own code**: the capability is defined, the enforcement site is absent | **`fixed`** | **FIXED 2026-08-26.** Ian settled part 2: *"signed in user = own org; for multi-store org up one step to store access / storeId linked to profile"* — and that axis already existed as `person_store_scope`, which `authorize()` has enforced for store-scoped capabilities since T-121. What shipped: **(1)** both pages go through `apps/erp/src/app/reports/lib.ts`'s `reportScope()` — `requireSession()`, org from `actor.orgId` (the `org` search param is DELETED, not ignored), stores filtered to `person_store_scope`, and every query under `withOrgPrincipal` so RLS is a real backstop. A store-scoped capability is authorized ONCE PER STORE the report covers, so someone scoped to two of five stores cannot reach an org-wide total by omitting the filter. **(2)** `tests/unit/no-ungated-read-surface.test.ts` — a new guard, proven to fire by reintroducing the exact defect. ⭐ **The structural lesson, worth more than the fix:** every *mutation* path in this repo is gated because `authorize()` sits inside the domain services that mutations must call — but a **read** path has no such choke point. It goes page → `getDb()` → SQL, touching no layer that has ever heard of an actor. Reports were built before T-138 and nothing went back for them. **Any read surface added from here needs its gate proven the way a guard is proven — by watching it refuse.** Verified closed: `GET /reports/trial-balance` and `/reports/income-statement` both return **307 to /sign-in**, and `?org=<another-org>` redirects too |
| **DEF-066** | ⚠️ | **Shareat System** | **The expense day-lock is now a MONTH-lock, and the cashier-cash distinction is gone.** v1 gates every expense create/edit/void on whether that DAY's sales report is verified, and specifically on whether the payment touches cashier-drawer cash — `expenseTouchesCashierCash()` plus `assertDayNotLockedServer` (`expense.actions.ts:126-139, 217-233`). v2 checks only that a **fiscal period** is `'open'`, inside `postEntry` (`post-entry.ts:111-120`); nothing under `packages/domain/src/expenses/` references sales days or verification at all. ⚠️ The control v1 protects is specific: once a cashier's day is counted and remitted, a new expense drawn against that day's till changes a figure somebody has already signed off. A month-level lock cannot see that — the day is closed but the period is open, so the write is allowed. **The coarsening is one level of granularity, and it is exactly the level the control operates at.** | `verified` | Belongs with T-136 (cash custody), where the sales-day/remittance relationship is modelled — not a bolt-on to expenses. ⚠️ Do NOT re-implement it as a second period check in the service layer; v1's version reads the sales day, which is the thing that actually knows. |
| **DEF-067** | 🔸 | **Shareat System** | **A payment split cannot carry a reference number or a note.** v1's `PaymentSplit` (`types/index.ts:507-519`) carries `referenceNo` and `notes` per split; v2's `expense_payments` (`schema/expenses.ts:128-165`) has `method, amount, account_code, staff_id, cash_request_id` and neither field. An online transfer's reference number is the one string that reconciles a line against a bank or e-wallet statement, and on a split it belongs to the LINE, not the expense — a three-way split has three different references.
