| **DEF-086** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **`undertimeGraceMinutes` BELOW 60 FORGIVES NOTHING, SILENTLY — a pay setting that cannot fire.** The shortfall it forgives is computed as `shortMinutes = (required − computedHours) × 60` (`session-hours.ts:112-129`), and `computedHours` is **always a whole number of hours**: the end comes from `graceAdjustedEndHour`, which returns `floored` or `floored + 1` (`shift-boundaries.ts:104-141`), and the start is either an allowed start **hour** or `Math.floor()` of the tap's hour (`session-hours.ts:103-105`). So against a whole-hour `requiredHoursPerShift` — which every real store has, the config's own comment says *“Lipa 10, Malvar 9”* — the shortfall is **always a multiple of 60**, and any grace of 1–59 fails `shortMinutes <= undertimeGrace` every time. ⚠️ **A manager who sets “forgive 15 minutes of undertime” gets no effect and no error**, and the setting reads as working. ⭐ Its sibling `earlyOutGraceMinutes` DOES work at sub-hour values, because it acts on the pre-floor minutes-into-the-hour — so the two controls sit side by side in the same settings block behaving completely differently, which is what makes this hard to notice. Found deriving T-157's engine, by writing the test v1's own doc comment implies and watching it fail | **`fixed`** | ✅ **FIXED in v2, 2026-08-29, on Ian's call** — *“about payroll its not yet implemented in v1 lots of work to do so we do it right and implement in full in v2.”* So option (b): **v2 pays REAL MINUTES and the hour-floor is gone** (`compute-day.ts`, `paidEndMinute`). The undertime grace then fires at any value, and the early-out grace keeps a distinct meaning — it credits a full shift when the departure is within the window, and pays exactly what was worked outside it. Closing tests: *“DEF-086 FIXED — a grace under 60 minutes now fires, because pay is in real minutes”* and the five `paidEndMinute` cases. ⭐ **The live data says this mattered: Lipa has `undertimeGraceMinutes: 15` set in production**, a policy that has never once fired. ⚠️ Superseded reasoning, kept for the record: The arithmetic is faithfully ported and the behaviour is **pinned by a named test** (`compute-day.test.ts`, *“DEF-086 — a grace under 60 minutes can never fire while hours are whole”*), so it cannot drift unnoticed again. Three options, and the choice moves real pay: **(a)** leave it, and make the settings UI refuse a value under 60 rather than accepting one that does nothing; **(b)** stop flooring the paid end to the hour, which makes the grace meaningful but restates every day's hours for every store; **(c)** allow a fractional `requiredHoursPerShift`, which makes it fire without touching the floor. ⭐ **(a) is the recommendation** — it costs nothing, changes nobody's pay, and turns a silent no-op into a visible refusal, which is the actual defect |
| **DEF-087** | ⚠️ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **TWO OF THREE OPERATING STORES RUN ATTENDANCE ON A CONFIG NOBODY SET.** Captured live 2026-08-29 (D4): **Lipa's `attendanceConfig` carries 22 keys; Malvar's carries 3 (`breakEnabled`, `enabledCredentials`, `shortShiftHalfDayOt`) and Banay-Banay's carries 1.** Everything absent falls back to `DEFAULT_ATTENDANCE_CONFIG` at READ time via v1's config merge — so Malvar and Banay-Banay are being paid against `requiredHoursPerShift: 9`, `allowedShiftStartHours: [9,10,11,14,15,16,17,18]`, `halfDayHours: 5` and every grace at 0, **none of which anyone chose for those stores.** ⚠️ The screen shows values, so it looks configured; the document does not contain them. ⭐ Lipa, by contrast, has real settings — `earlyOutGraceMinutes: 30`, `undertimeGraceMinutes: 15`, `lateGraceMinutes: 5`, `latePolicy: charge_per_minute` at ₱1/min — which is what makes the comparison stark rather than theoretical. ⛔ **A store's allowed start hours are the single most load-bearing setting in the module** (they decide every anchor, so every day's paid hours), and two stores have never had one | `verified` | ✅ **Structurally impossible in v2, and it was closed before this was found.** `store_attendance_config` (T-155, migration 0081) is **NOT NULL on every policy column with a default**, so “unset” cannot exist — the exact reasoning `stores.remittanceCadence` already carries (T-152): *“an unset cadence is the same defect class as an unmapped expense category — it fails quietly, in the direction that looks fine.”* A v2 store either has a config row or has none at all, and `getLiveConfig` refuses the second case by name rather than inventing defaults. ⚠️ **What Ian still has to do is OPERATIONAL, not technical:** decide what Malvar and Banay-Banay's real hours and start times are, because migrating them will otherwise import a default as though it were a decision |
| **DEF-088** | ⛔ | ERP-rebuild | ⭐ **v1 PAYS FOR SHIFTS NOBODY WORKED, AND NOTHING FLAGS THEM — 40.4 EXCESS HOURS IN ONE MONTH ACROSS TWO STORES.** Measured over the full August capture (550 paired sessions, D4): **12 sessions were credited more than TWICE the time between their first and last tap, and all twelve are `auto_paired`** — no anomaly, no review, straight into payroll. The worst: **`lipa-0319` present 1.6 minutes, paid 5 hours**; `malvar-0003` present **18 seconds**, paid 4 hours; `lipa-0245` present **8.9 seconds**, paid 2 hours. ⚠️ **No single rule is wrong — it is the COMBINATION of two deliberate ones.** A late arrival is credited from the preceding allowed start (v1 pays the full shift and recovers lateness through the CHARGE, not through hours — `shift-boundaries.ts:185-190`), and the paid end floors to the hour. Apply both to a double-tap seconds apart and the day pays from a shift start hours earlier to the current hour. ⛔ The 8-second debounce does not catch it (`lipa-0245` is 8.9 seconds), and the pairer takes first and last tap of the day — so if those are the only two taps, the “shift” is the gap between them. ⭐ Found by the golden master on real data, which is exactly what D4 was approved for | **`fixed`** | ✅ **CLOSED STRUCTURALLY 2026-08-29, and a better fix than the ratio guard alone.** Ian, given the exact 8.9-second scenario: *“that must be not counted at all min half day 5 hours, 1><5 hour record as unverified OT to be accepted by manager.”* ⭐ **The DAY stops being a unit a double-tap can manufacture.** Under one hour credits **nothing at all**; one hour to the half day credits **no day** and raises the whole stint as **unverified overtime a manager must accept**; only at the half day does a day exist again. The session that credited two hours for nine seconds now credits **zero**, and what it raises must pass a human before it is money. `MINIMUM_CREDITABLE_MINUTES = 60`; `halfDayMinutes` above it stays per-store config. ⚠️ **The ratio guard is kept and was TIGHTENED as a direct consequence** — it now weighs pay **plus pending overtime** against presence, because the bands route a short stint entirely into OT and a guard reading `computedMinutes` alone would have seen zero and gone quiet on exactly the shape it exists for. **Superseded reasoning, kept for the record:** ⚠️ v2 is NOT automatically safe: paying real minutes (DEF-086) removes the end-flooring half, but the anchor half is a policy we deliberately keep, so a 30-second double tap at 20:09 against an 18:00 start would still credit two hours. So `computeDay` raises a new **`implausible_span`** anomaly when a day is credited more than `MAX_CREDIT_TO_PRESENCE_RATIO` (**2**) times the raw presence, and `adjudicateDay` treats it as BLOCKING — the day lands `needs_review` and can never auto-pay. It **flags rather than refuses**, because a genuine late arrival paid from a scheduled start is legitimate and only a manager can tell the difference. ⚠️ **The ratio needs Ian.** `2` is a deliberately loose first cut chosen so no honest late arrival is swept up — all twelve real cases are far past it. It is a named exported constant so it can be argued with. ⛔ **And the v1 side is untouched and still live**: this closes v2, not the ₱-per-month leak running today |
| **DEF-089** | ⚠️ | **Shareat System** | ⭐ **NO DEFAULT PRIVILEGE FOR FUTURE SEQUENCES — the app role cannot use a `serial` column added after migration 0011.** `0011_row_level_security.sql:66-72` grants `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON ALL TABLES` **and** sets `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … ON TABLES`, so every table created afterwards inherits its grants automatically. For sequences it grants `USAGE, SELECT ON ALL SEQUENCES` — the ones that existed **at that moment** — and sets **no default privilege at all**. ⚠️ **Undetected across 67 migrations** because every table since has used `uuidv7()` defaults and needed no sequence. ⛔ The first `bigserial` after 0011 is T-181's `mirror_outbox`, and it failed on its first insert with `permission denied for sequence mirror_outbox_id_seq` — from a TRIGGER, so the error surfaces on whatever business write happened to fire it rather than anywhere near the cause. Anyone adding a serial column in any module would have hit the same wall. ⭐ Found by building T-181, not by auditing 0011 | **`fixed`** | Migration `0088` grants both new sequences explicitly **and** adds the `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … GRANT USAGE, SELECT ON SEQUENCES` that 0011 omitted, so the next one just works. ⚠️ **The fix rides along with T-181 rather than getting its own migration**, deliberately: the grant is meaningless without a sequence to grant on, and splitting it would leave a migration whose only effect is invisible until something else lands. ⭐ The lesson is the shape, not the grant: **`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` is per object TYPE**, and 0011 covered tables while quietly leaving sequences, functions and types to whoever noticed next |
| **DEF-090** | ⛔ | **Shareat System** | ⭐ **THE INGESTOR'S “ONE BAD EVENT NEVER STOPS THE BATCH” WAS FALSE FOR EVERY DATABASE ERROR — one tablet sending a malformed id would have taken a whole store's day of punches down.** `ingestEvents` (T-182) wraps each event in a `try`/`catch` and quarantines whatever throws, and its own header promises *“one bad event never stops the batch, because a single malformed document from one tablet must not hold up a whole store's day.”* ⚠️ **That held only for LOGIC errors.** A bad `method`, an unknown terminal or a disabled credential is decided in TypeScript before any statement runs, so the catch worked and all nine T-182 tests exercised exactly that class. A value that is the right JSON **type** but not a uuid — `employmentId: "y"` — reaches Postgres, which then **aborts the entire transaction**: the catch-all's own quarantine `INSERT` was refused too (`current transaction is aborted`), and `ingestEvents` **threw instead of quarantining**. ⛔ So the batch died, every later event in it was lost, and the failure the ingestor exists to prevent was hiding inside the code written to prevent it. ⭐ Found building T-183 — by a bug in my own test that happened to double-encode a uuid, then reproduced deliberately with a one-event probe | **`fixed`** | Each event now runs in its own **savepoint** (`tx.transaction()`, which on Postgres is a nested transaction → savepoint), so a failing statement rolls back that event alone and the outer transaction is usable again on the next line — which is what lets the quarantine write actually happen. ⚠️ **The first fix attempt was hand-written `savepoint`/`rollback to savepoint` statements and it did NOT work**: postgres.js manages transaction scope itself and rolls back on a rejected query, so the driver's own savepoint API had to be used rather than raw SQL. Pinned by a failing-first test in T-182's own file — *“⭐ 9. a DATABASE error in one event is quarantined, and the rest of the batch still lands”* — which asserts the poison event quarantines as `internal_error` **and** the two healthy events after it still become punches. ⭐ The lesson is about test design, not savepoints: **nine tests covered “a bad event” and every one of them was bad in the same way.** A batch-resilience claim needs a failure from each LAYER — validation, database, and network — not three more from the layer that was easiest to write |
| **DEF-091** | ⛔ | **Shareat System** | ⭐ **A RESERVED MIGRATION BLOCK RESERVED THE NUMBERS BUT NOT THE TIMESTAMPS, SO DRIZZLE SILENTLY SKIPPED A MIGRATION ON THE SHARED DEV DATABASE.** Phase 4's lane was given migrations **0070-0089** and `shareat_test_b`; the accounting lane on `main` holds 0060 onward. Both lanes hand-write `meta/_journal.json` (DEF-073) and both step `when` by **one day per migration** from the last shared entry, 0059. ⚠️ **Both are internally consistent and both pass every journal test** — but the accounting lane reached idx **70** (`0070_t172_branch_orders`, taking a number inside this lane's reservation) at *exactly* the millisecond this lane had given ITS 0070. ⛔ Drizzle applies a migration only when `lastApplied.created_at < this.when`, so on the shared `shareat_dev` **this lane's 0070 was skipped without a word** and 0071 then failed with *"relation `employments` does not exist"* — an error pointing at the migration AFTER the one that actually went missing. ⭐ This is DEF-022's failure a second time, in a new costume: the 2026-08-23 incident was one lane generating a lower `when`; this is two lanes generating the SAME one. ⭐ Found running `npm run db:migrate` to smoketest T-183, not by auditing the journal | **`fixed`** | ⭐ **The rule is now `when` must advance at least as fast as `idx`** — skip ten numbers, skip ten days — which makes a reserved INDEX block automatically a reserved `when` block, so two lanes that honour their number reservations cannot collide on timestamps either, in any merge order. Enforced by a new test in `packages/db/tests/migration-journal.test.ts`. ⚠️ **The two existing journal tests could not have caught this and are not at fault:** both are *within-journal* checks, and the colliding entry lived in another branch's journal entirely. This lane's block moved **0070-0089 → 0080-0089** (ten `git mv`s, journal rewritten, every reference updated), `shareat_test_b` was dropped and rebuilt, and both databases re-migrated clean — 49 integration files / 1343 tests green afterwards. ⚠️ **Two things are Ian's, not fixable here:** `main` has taken **0070**, inside a block it was told not to use, so the reservation was either not communicated or not honoured; and the two lanes have also collided on TASK numbers — `main`'s T-181/T-182/T-183 are stock locations, stock ledger and stock layers, while this lane's are the Lane A publisher, the Lane B ingestor and this screen. **Six documents now describe two different things each.** |
| **DEF-175** | ⚠️ | ⭐ **A SCHEMA DOC COMMENT DESCRIBED THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THE CODE WRITES, ON A MONEY COLUMN.** `packages/db/schema/trade-discrepancies.ts` documented `trade_discrepancies.value` as *"Quantity × the line's transfer rate"* from T-174 (2026-08-30) until 2026-09-05. The code writes it **at COST** — `packages/domain/src/commissary/transfer.ts:423-429`, whose own comment is emphatic and correct: *"AT COST, because that is what is actually stranded in `1340`. ⚠️ Valuing the dispute at the transfer rate would make every disposal relieve `1340` by more than the goods put into it."* The column, the constraint and the arithmetic are all right; **only the sentence a reader uses to write new arithmetic was wrong** | **`fixed`** | Comment corrected 2026-09-05 under T-212, which is also how it was found: T-212's first draft of `receivedInRange` trusted the comment and cross-checked a transfer-rate figure against `value`, a reconciliation that could never have tied. Confirmed empirically on dev data the same day — a 10 kg shortfall on a ₱310/kg line at ₱300/kg cost carries `value = 3000.00`, not `3100.00`. **No code change: nothing was computing from the comment, and the one thing that nearly did is this task.** ⚠️ This is precisely the hazard `packages/db/schema/trade.ts:115-125` already names for this same table (DEF-095) — *"a constraint changed in SQL and not mirrored back into this file is invisible until someone trusts the file"* — recurring here as prose rather than as a constraint. **The lesson is narrower than "keep comments current": a comment stating a UNIT or a BASIS for a money column is load-bearing, and belongs with the code that writes the value, not only beside the column that stores it.** To reconcile a shortfall against a transfer-rate figure, multiply `trade_discrepancies.quantity` by the LINE's `rate` |
| **DEF-176** | 🔸 | **`receivingDisputeOverdue` was assigned to T-213 by the phase-6 plan and does not exist.** `docs/plans/phase-6-reporting-and-dashboards.md` §6 Q3 states Phase 6's contribution to receiving-dispute ageing is *"the **detector** (`receivingDisputeOverdue`, T-213) — which makes the ageing visible either way"*, and closes Ian's open question on that basis. T-213 shipped eight detectors on 2026-09-05; this is not one of them (`grep` over `packages/db/src/repo/findings.ts` confirms its absence). The gap is invisible because **both tasks are marked done** — the plan believes T-213 carried it and T-213's own spec never listed it | **`fixed`** | **Live exposure it was meant to cover:** 4 of 9 open receiving disputes already exceed 15 days (plan §6 Q3, live-probed 2026-09-04), against a v1 code comment arguing no backstop is needed because *"disputes get resolved."* ⭐ **Partly mitigated the same day by T-212**, which puts the AGE on the dashboard and satisfies the phase's own exit criterion (§9 item 9) — but a dashboard tile is not a finding: it is not severity-ordered, cannot be acknowledged, and does not age visibly in `/findings`. **Owner: whoever picks up T-214 or a T-213 follow-up.** ⚠️ Note the detector must use `(now() at time zone 'Asia/Manila')::date`, not bare `current_date` — DEF-172 **✅ BUILT 2026-09-05 by T-218.** `receivingDisputeOverdue` (`findings.ts`), threshold 15 days, `soft`, per commissary store, registered in `findingsFor` — ⛔ registration checked explicitly, because a detector that exists and is not called is `reference_shareat_done_but_uncalled` exactly. Its set is character-for-character `tradeDiscrepanciesRepo.unresolved`’s predicate (`status <> 'resolved'`), a fourth consumer and not a fourth definition. ⚠️ A **deferred** dispute still fires and its deferral is named in the detail — *“a promise to decide by a date, not an exemption”* (T-174). Amount is labelled **at cost** (DEF-175). Five tests: fires past 15 days, silent inside it, silent once resolved however old, fires when deferred, and an exact-boundary pair (15 not overdue, 16 overdue). Verified by breaking it both ways — unregistering the detector fails exactly the 3 positive tests, and flipping `>` to `>=` fails the boundary test alone. |
| **DEF-178** | 🔸 | **`lane-health.test.ts`'s terminal-silence test mixes two clocks at an exact boundary, and reddens the suite under load.** T-183 invariant 6 sets `last_seen_at = now() - interval '30 hours'` using **Postgres**'s clock, then asserts `silentHours` is exactly `30` — but `silentHours` is `Math.floor((now - lastSeenAt)/3_600_000)` computed in **JavaScript** (`packages/domain/src/sync/health.ts:206-210`) against a JS `Date`. The two clocks need only disagree by a millisecond in the wrong direction for the quotient to fall to `29.999…` and floor to **29** | **`fixed`** | Observed 2026-09-05 in a full integration run on a loaded machine: `AssertionError: expected 29 to be 30`. **Re-running the same file alone passed 21/21**, which is what identifies it as a boundary flake rather than a break — and it is a flake that will redden CI at random, where the natural response is to re-run rather than to look. ⚠️ **Not caused by, and unrelated to, T-211/T-212** (the failing file imports neither). **Suggested fix — do not simply widen the assertion to `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(30)`**, which hides the same class of bug in `terminalStatus` itself: stamp the fixture from the SAME clock the assertion uses (pass an explicit `now` into `health()`, as the inbox test above it already does with `reportedAt`), so the test stops depending on two clocks agreeing. Owner: whoever holds the T-183 lane **✅ FIXED 2026-09-06 (T-223's tail), after it reddened a real gate.** It failed a full-suite run during a push — `expected 29 to be 30` — while passing 21/21 with the file run alone, which is the signature that makes it read as a regression and get re-run rather than looked at. ⭐ **The fix is one clock, injected**: `laneHealth` already accepts `now`, so the fixture stamps `last_seen_at` from a JavaScript `Date` and the same instant is passed in. No skew is possible. ⛔ **Deliberately NOT widened to `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(30)`** — that hides the same class of bug inside `terminalStatus`, which is what the assertion exists to catch. ⚠️ **And a calibration finding worth keeping**: probing the repaired test showed the assertion is COARSE. Changing `Math.floor` to `Math.ceil` does not fail it (at an exact 30.0 they agree), and neither does a 3% error in the divisor (30.86 still floors to 30) — only a change large enough to move the integer does. An integer-hours assertion tolerates just under an hour of arithmetic error, which is fine for an outage alarm and should not be mistaken for precision. |
| **DEF-179** | 🔸 | **A repo-scanning guard silently scanned nothing, because this repo checks out CRLF and JavaScript's `.` does not match a carriage return.** CR is a line terminator in JS regex, exactly like LF — so on a CRLF line `/--.*$/` can never reach end-of-string: `.*` stops before the CR and `$` is not there yet. A comment-stripping helper built on `/\/\/.*$/` and `/--.*$/` therefore returned **every line unchanged**, and the guard read comments as if they were code. ⚠️ **In a stripper the failure is loud** (false positives) — but the identical bug in a MATCHER is silent: any `.*$`-anchored scan over a CRLF checkout matches nothing and passes. Found 2026-09-05 building T-218's guard, which flagged its own explanatory comment. ⚠️ Hit again minutes later editing this register: its rows end with CRLF, so a script searching for a bare LF found nothing | **`fixed`** | Normalise CRLF to LF before any line-oriented regex — one line, with the reason beside it. ⭐ **The two existing grep-shaped guards were checked and are SAFE**: `tests/unit/no-client-supplied-actor.test.ts` uses unanchored substring patterns (no `.*$`), and `packages/db/tests/integration/migration-journal.test.ts` only regexes filenames. So this is a trap for the NEXT such guard, not a live hole. ⚠️ Git prints "LF will be replaced by CRLF" on essentially every commit here, so the condition is permanent on a Windows checkout. ⭐ **And this row is its own third instance**: written with real control characters instead of the escape TEXT, it shattered into **nine** physical lines and rendered as garbage from the moment it was filed until T-220 repaired it. ⚠️ **Why it survived a day unnoticed is the transferable half**: the author only ever read it in the diff they wrote, never rendered. **Reading your own artefact in the form you authored it is not verification** — the same reason DEF-183 survived two weeks, where reading the policy reassured and only running it refused. |
| **DEF-177** | 🔸 | **`setPin`'s pre-check only refuses a PIN already taken by a DIFFERENT person — re-entering the SAME person's own current PIN reaches the database's unique index instead, which raises a raw Postgres error (`duplicate key value violates unique constraint "person_credentials_lookup_hash_unique"`) straight to the screen.** `packages/auth/src/sessions.ts`'s `setPin`: `if (taken && taken.personId !== input.personId) throw ...` — when `taken.personId === input.personId` the check passes silently and `insertCredential` proceeds to the `INSERT`, which the unique index on `lookup_hash` then rejects. Found live-smoketesting T-217's PIN-issuance UI: re-submitting a person's own already-issued PIN through the new "Re-issue PIN" form surfaced this exact raw message | `fixed` | ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-08 — T-237 slice C, as the visible half of DEF-228**: `setPin` now retires the person's own live PIN for that store before inserting, so the same digits re-issue cleanly and this path is reached only by a race (two administrators issuing the same digits at the same store in the same moment — the pre-check reads before either insert). The mapping is **kept** for that race and re-worded to what actually happened: *"That PIN was just taken at this store — try another."* (`staff/actions.ts:54-67`). History of the mapping, as it was: `errorMessage()` recognised `person_credentials_lookup_hash_unique` and returned *"That's already this person's current PIN — nothing changed. Pick a different PIN to re-issue."* — which was true of the same-person case and is now never that case. `setPin` itself was untouched at the time (out of T-217's Scope — see that task's own Do-Not-Touch list); the real fix belongs with whoever next changes `setPin`: either treat `taken.personId === input.personId` as a same-value no-op, or extend the pre-check's condition to cover it so the friendly message is generated at the source instead of pattern-matched from a driver error one layer up. Any other caller of `setPin` reached the same way (none exist yet — DEF-174) would hit the same raw leak until then |

The deeper cause is `docs/RULES.md`'s own row 47 (*"parallel agents must share no STATE"*) being true of `shareat_test` and quietly untrue of `shareat_dev` |
| **DEF-095** | ⛔ | **Shareat System** | **`packages/db/schema/trade.ts` stated a constraint the database had stopped enforcing, and the stale text caused a migration to REVERT a shipped fix.** The file carried T-132's `trade_orders_dispatch_shape_check` — `ar_charge_id IS NOT NULL` on every dispatch — for a full day after **T-133 replaced it in migration `0054` on 2026-08-28** with a `journal_entry_id` requirement covering `dispatched` *and* `received`, exactly so an internal transfer (which raises no receivable) could dispatch. Only the migration was updated. T-172 read the schema file, concluded the constraint blocked internal orders, and wrote `0070` to drop and re-add it — **silently undoing 0054's fix**. Caught by 9 failing tests in `packages/domain/tests/integration/trade.test.ts` (T-133's own suite), not by review | **`fixed`** | `0070` no longer touches the constraint and says why in a comment; `schema/trade.ts` now mirrors 0054's actual text with the history in its docblock. ⭐ **The general defect is the one worth keeping:** migrations here are **hand-written** (DEF-073), so nothing regenerates `schema/` from SQL and nothing compares them. A constraint changed in a migration and not mirrored back leaves the schema — *the file everyone reads first, and the one `docs/RULES.md` points at for “adding a table”* — describing rules the database does not have. ⚠️ **A drift check is cheap and absent**: dump `pg_constraint` for every table in `schema/` and diff against the drizzle definitions. Until it exists, **read the database, not the schema file, before changing a constraint**. ⭐ What `0070` legitimately adds is the opposite direction — `trade_orders_internal_no_ar_check`: an internal order **cannot** carry a receivable, so v1's branch-as-customer mistake (the one `excludeBranchSales` exists to paper over) is unrepresentable rather than merely un-written |

The map's own comment says the kind is *"written by T-176's conversion"*, so this is a known-forward gap rather than an oversight — recorded because the map reads as complete | `reported` | **T-176 (production and prep conversion) must add `prepped` to `ITEM_KINDS`** in the same change that first produces one, or its output will be indistinguishable from raw material. ⚠️ Until then every commissary transfer lands in `1310`/`1320`, which is correct for what can currently exist and will silently stay correct-looking after T-176 ships if the kind is forgotten — the account simply stays at zero and nothing says why |

| **DEF-098** | ⛔ | **Shareat System** | **`1340 Inventory in transit` could never clear: debited at COST, credited at the TRANSFER RATE.** T-172's dispatch debits `1340` with what the goods cost the commissary (FIFO, ₱300/kg); T-173's receipt credited it at the agreed transfer price (₱310/kg). Those differ *by design* — T-133: *"the transfer PRICE carries the processing cost."* So a fully received ₱30,000 shipment transferred at ₱31,000 left `1340` at **−₱1,000, permanently, once per shipment**, and a short receipt left a residual that was wrong in the other direction. ⚠️ **The trial balance still tied** — the ₱1,000 was merely in the wrong account — so nothing announced it; the periodic pattern it replaced had no in-transit step and therefore no place for the error to hide. Found by T-174's fixture asking what was actually stranded after a 90-of-100 receipt, one day after the shape shipped | **`fixed`** | ⭐ **The rule: `1340` is a COST account, relieved at cost, always.** The margin is a separate fact — the commissary's processing work — credited to **`5175 Cost transferred to branches`** and **only for goods that actually arrive**. `trade_order_lines.dispatch_cost` (migration `0071`) carries what left, so the receipt reads it back rather than being told; `proportionOfCost` splits it on exact integer centavos, returning the dispatch cost **unchanged** on a full receipt so the account provably clears. ⭐ **This makes `5175` load-bearing under perpetual rather than a periodic leftover** — T-165 §4 keeps it for the franchise case; this is a second and closer reason, and it is the elimination role `5170`'s comment has demanded since T-102b (commissary P&L nets to zero: an internal transfer earns nothing). Closing tests: `commissary-transfer.test.ts` (1340 clears to `0.00` on a full receipt, `5175` at −1,000; ₱3,000 **at cost** stranded on a short one) and `commissary-discrepancy.test.ts`'s **two-tie gate**. ⚠️ **The gate had to be split to state this properly**: `1310+1320+1330 = open layer value`, and `1340 = unresolved discrepancy value` — goods on a truck are deliberately in NO layer, and a `1340` balance with no unresolved row to explain it is the ghost worth catching |
| **DEF-099** | ⚠️ | **Shareat System** | **Nothing in the codebase ever CREDITS `1230 Advances to employees` — the staff receivable can only grow.** Three modules debit it: `owners/index.ts:429` (party `employee`), `cash-requests/index.ts:371` (a short remittance charged to the cashier) and now T-174's `charge` disposition. A search of the whole tree finds **no credit, no document and no month-end step** — the payroll deduction that draws it down is Phase 5 and does not exist. ⭐ **Structurally identical to DEF-082** (`2160` service-charge float): the accrual shipped, the clearing did not, and the account reads as a healthy growing asset the whole time | `verified` | **T-174 does NOT gate on this, deliberately, and the reasoning is worth keeping.** Its spec's invariant 6 said a `charge` should be refused while Phase 5 is unbuilt — written before the source pass. ⛔ Refusing would leave the only options `audit` and `loss`, so a genuinely **recoverable** shortfall gets written off as a loss, which is worse and also irreversible in the books. And the same receivable is already raisable through two shipped doors, so a gate here would be inconsistent rather than protective. ⭐ **The right control is an ALARM, not a block**, exactly as DEF-082 concluded: a `staffAdvanceAgeing` detector in `repo/findings.ts` that fires while `1230` has a balance older than one payroll cycle. ⚠️ It is not built. Until Phase 5 lands, a charged shortfall is a receivable nobody collects |

| **DEF-100** | 🔸 | **Shareat System** | **`ITEM_KINDS` has no `equipment`, so three specs' "equipment is exempt" rules guard nothing — and one of them would have been written as code.** T-175 §6.3 and T-168 both exempt *"equipment-kind items"* from thresholds, auto-order and usage. `packages/db/schema/items.ts:47` defines the kinds as `raw_material, supply, retail, meat_slab, meat_sliced`; equipment lives in `fixed_assets`, a different table with a different lifecycle. ⭐ The exemption is therefore **already structural** — an equipment item cannot be an `items` row at all — and writing the check would have added a branch that can never be true | `verified` | **No code, by design; recorded so the next reader stops looking for the check.** ⚠️ The residual risk is the reverse one: if `equipment` is ever added to `ITEM_KINDS` (T-176's `prepped` is a pending example — DEF-096), these exemptions silently become real requirements with nothing implementing them. **Any addition to `ITEM_KINDS` must be checked against every spec that names a kind**, the same way DEF-036's lesson applies to statuses: *a guard written against the states that exist today silently stops guarding when a new state is introduced.* |
| **DEF-108** | ⚠️ | **An owner's drawing cap will not be freed by the payroll that recovers it.** `ownersRepo.drawnInWindow` counts `1230` lines for the owner's person WHERE `je.source_id = owner.id` — scoped that way on purpose, so an ordinary staff advance does not consume an OWNERSHIP cap (a browser check caught exactly that: a ₱600 shortage silently eating a drawing limit). ⚠️ **The owners module left a note for this exact moment**: *“when payroll lands, the entry that nets one of these off must carry the same source_id, or the recovered amount will keep consuming the cap forever.”* T-189's payslip entry carries `source_id = {payslipId}`, so a recovery it nets off is invisible to that query and the cap stays consumed. | Open | ⛔ **Not fixed inside T-189, and not by giving the payslip a second source_id** — one entry cannot carry two, and a payslip may net advances from several origins. ⭐ The fix belongs in the owners query: count the NET of `1230` for that person's owner-recovery rather than debits scoped by `source_id`. It needs the owners lane's eyes, because the scoping it would relax is the thing that stopped a staff shortage consuming a drawing limit. ⚠️ Only bites when an owner is also a paid employment |
| **DEF-109** | ⛔ | **T-189 paid PENDING overtime that nobody had approved.** `attendance_days.pending_ot_minutes` carries a warning written by the attendance lane and addressed to Phase 5 in as many words: *“Recorded, never paid here — the approval gate is Phase 5, and auto-paying it is exactly what the pending status exists to prevent.”* T-189's `previewPayRun` read it straight into `otMinutes` and paid it, so every pending minute became money the moment a run posted. ⚠️ **The comment was addressed to the module that ignored it** — no test could catch this, because the tests were written by the same author under the same misunderstanding. | Fixed | T-189e added the gate the comment names: a sixth adjustment kind `overtime_approved` carrying `approved_ot_minutes`. Payroll now pays `min(approved, worked)`, and `null` (nobody decided) pays NOTHING. ⭐ Unapproved overtime is SURFACED on `/payroll` rather than silently dropped — an unpaid hour that vanishes from the screen is indistinguishable from an hour nobody worked. |
| **DEF-110** | ⚠️ | **v1 applies the SAME late grace two different ways, in one file.** `attendance-review.actions.ts:461` treats it as a CUTOFF — `lateMinutes > grace ? lateMinutes : 0`, so over the allowance the FULL duration is filed — while `:828` treats it as a DEDUCTION — `max(0, lateMinutes - grace)`, so only the excess is. The money path (`compute.ts` `chargeableLateMinutes`) uses the CUTOFF. ⚠️ **So with a 15-minute grace and somebody 20 minutes late, v1 charges their PAY for 20 minutes and files their INFRACTION for 5.** Two numbers for one lateness, on one day, in one system. ⛔ The cutoff is also a cliff in its own right: 15 minutes late costs nothing, 16 minutes costs SIXTEEN. | Open | ⚠️ **Not ported — v2 has no late charge yet** (T-189 §4 deliberately left it out; it changes the payslip identity and needs its own task). Recorded now because the port must not carry the inconsistency across. ⭐ The fix when it lands: ONE function, and make cutoff-vs-deduct a per-store SETTING defaulting to deduct — the cliff becomes a choice rather than an accident. |
| **DEF-106** | ⚠️ | **A supplier could be created and never edited.** `apRepo.updateSupplier` was written in full — org-scoped, row-checked, throwing when the id does not exist — and had **no caller anywhere**: no domain service, no server action, no screen. A typo in a supplier's name, a wrong VAT status or a changed contact number was therefore permanent. ⚠️ **The same shape as the `count_sessions` near-miss (T-169) and supplier returns**: a write path that exists and is unreachable looks identical to a working one in every review that reads the repo layer. | Fixed | T-188 added `editSupplier` (gated `approve_supplier`, the same capability as create — a name and a bank account decide where money goes) and an edit form on `/ap/suppliers/[id]`. ⭐ **The cheapest possible win**: the write path was already correct and merely unwired. |
| **DEF-107** | ⚠️ | **Two main-nav entries pointed at routes that do not exist.** `‘Suppliers’ → /ap/suppliers` and `‘Supplier invoices’ → /ap/invoices` were both in the TRADE group, and neither had a `page.tsx` — only `[id]/page.tsx`. Both 404’d. ⚠️ `nav.tsx`’s own docblock justifies the nav existing so that pages are not unreachable; this is the inverse failure, and the build output shows it plainly (`├ ƒ /ap/suppliers/[id]` with no parent). | Fixed | T-188 built `/ap/suppliers` as the supplier register and pointed `Supplier invoices` at `/ap`, which lists them already. ⚠️ **A route in the nav needs a build-output check, not a glance at the folder** — a `[id]` directory makes the parent look present. |
| **DEF-104** | ⛔ | **DEF-039's damage is PERMANENT on any database migrated during the broken window, and `migrate` cannot repair it.** DEF-039 records that a migration can be silently skipped when `when` does not rise with `idx`. This is what that leaves behind. Found 2026-08-30: `shareat_test` had **73 of 87** migrations applied and **zero attendance tables** — all ten attendance migrations (idx 80–89) plus three inventory ones (70–72) carried a `when` BELOW the highest already recorded, so the runner skipped them and will skip them forever. ⚠️ **187 integration tests failed against a green working tree**, and the failure looked like broken code rather than a broken database. ⭐ Fixing the journal fixes only FRESH databases; an existing one must be **dropped and recreated**, which is the part nobody would guess. | Fixed | Both local databases rebuilt from scratch (87/87). ⛔ **The lesson is the remedy, not the rebuild**: after ANY journal-timestamp correction, every already-migrated database is suspect and must be checked, not assumed. Compare `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations` against `_journal.json` — a `when` present in the journal but absent from the table, and below the table's maximum, is an unrepairable hole. |
| **DEF-105** | ⚠️ | **`remittance.shortage` is a documented posting pattern that nothing implements.** `POSTING-PATTERNS.md` §`remittance.shortage` specifies `Dr 1230 / Cr 1110` for a batch shortage charged to a cashier, and `resolveRemittanceVariance` **only flips a status** — it posts nothing. Verified 2026-08-30: no module under `packages/domain/src/remittance/` references `1230` at all. So a variance can be marked `charged` against a named person while the ledger shows no receivable from them, and the money is simply gone from the books. | Open | ⛔ **Not fixed inside T-186 on purpose.** T-186 posts the equivalent charge for a COUNTED shortage, and adding a second one here would put two cash-shortage posting paths in two modules — the drift the bible warns about (v1's two amortisation runners). ⭐ When it is fixed, both should call ONE helper. |
| **DEF-111** | ⛔ | ⭐ **A DAY THAT CREDITS ZERO MINUTES IS PAID A FULL DAILY RATE.** `computePay`'s `unitsFor` (`packages/domain/src/payroll/compute.ts:133`) reads **only `isHalfDay`** — it returns `1` for every day that is not a half day, and nothing anywhere filters `computedMinutes === 0`. `compute-day.ts:557-576` is explicit that below the half-day bar *"there is NO DAY"*: it sets `computedMinutes = 0`, leaves `isHalfDay` **false**, and routes the time to pending overtime. Payroll then pays that day in full. ⛔ **Replayed over the live 560-session golden corpus: 31 sessions credit zero minutes and every one is paid a whole day** — three of them are sessions of **under five minutes**. This is DEF-088's own shape (credit far exceeding presence) defeated one layer downstream: attendance closed the door, payroll left the window open, and `resolveStatus` marks these days `computed`, so they are payable. ⚠️ Present in **both** `shortShiftHalfDayOt` states — the flag does not touch this branch. Third occurrence of the DEF-109 pattern: *a comment addressed to a future module is only as good as that module reading it* | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **T-194, 2026-08-31.** `unitsFor` returns `0` when `computedMinutes <= 0`. ⛔ **And the half that is not arithmetic:** such a day is *entirely* pending overtime, so paying it ₱0 and **freezing** it would have put that overtime permanently beyond `recordAdjustment` — destroying the stint rather than deferring it. `previewPayRun` therefore **holds** a zero-credit day on which nobody has decided: not paid, **not frozen**, counted as `heldZeroCreditDays` and surfaced on `/payroll`. ⚠️ `approvedOtMinutes === 0` is a DECISION and is not held. ⭐ **Frappe HR's configurable half-day fraction was deliberately NOT adopted** — v1 hardcodes 0.5 with a documented reason and no store has ever wanted another value; a knob nobody turns is scope creep. Closing tests: `compute.test.ts` *"a zero-minute day contributes NO day"* and `payroll.test.ts` *"the held day is paid by the NEXT run once approved — nothing was lost"*. Smoketested on 9008: a posted run froze all ten paid days and left the zero-credit day and its 240 pending minutes untouched |
| **DEF-112** | ⛔ | ⭐ **WITH `shortShiftHalfDayOt` OFF — THE v2 DEFAULT — BASIC PAY IS COMPLETELY DECOUPLED FROM HOURS WORKED.** `isHalfDay` is set in exactly one place: the short-shift branch, which is inert while the flag is false (a manager override is the only other route, and it has no UI — DEF-113). So `unitsFor` returns `1` for **every** payable day and basic pay reduces to *headcount × daily rate*. ⛔ **Measured on the live corpus (560 sessions, 3 stores, 29 days): flag OFF pays `560.0` days; flag ON pays `508.5`. At an illustrative ₱500 daily rate that is ₱280,000 against ₱254,250 — ₱25,750, or 9.2% of basic payroll, in one month.** ⚠️ The consequence is that the whole of T-157's adjudication engine — anchors, graces, break deductions, undertime — influences nothing but the overtime and night-differential premiums. A 5-hour shift and a 9-hour shift pay identically. ⭐ **And the live evidence says the flag is not optional:** the raw capture shows all three operating stores (Lipa, Malvar, Banay-Banay) carry `shortShiftHalfDayOt: true` in v1 today, against a v1 default of `false` (`ERP-rebuild/src/types/attendance-config.ts:205`) — so each set it deliberately. v2 defaulting it `false` **reverses live policy** rather than preserving it | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **T-197, 2026-08-31.** Migration `0111` flips the column DEFAULT to `true`, which is the whole fix for a system that *"always assumes a ZERO starting point"*: every store is created fresh and `seedAttendanceConfig` inserts with no explicit values, so the default IS the policy. ⛔ **The first draft tried to `update` existing rows and T-155's immutability trigger refused it by name** — *"supersede with a new row instead of editing"* — which is the better design and now recorded in T-197 §2.1: a config row is effective-dated because T-157 invariant 11 judges a day by the config that governed IT. An existing store is superseded through `supersedeConfig`, on a date somebody picks; ⭐ and that service **also refuses to back-date over already-computed days** (*"would silently restate what those days paid"*), which is the same principle as the payroll freeze. Verified in the browser: a 7-hour duty now renders as **5h ½ paid + 2h pending** — Ian's model, live. ⚠️ Any store created in a v2 database before today keeps `false` until superseded |
| **DEF-113** | ⛔ | ⭐ **THE ENTIRE MANAGER ADJUSTMENT LAYER IS UNREACHABLE — `recordAdjustment` HAS NO CALLER OUTSIDE TESTS.** T-158 shipped six adjustment kinds (`start_anchor`, `early_out_approved`, `half_day_override`, `verify`, `void`, `overtime_approved`), fully tested, and **no screen or server action anywhere in `apps/erp` invokes any of them** — `/attendance/lane` is the quarantine/health screen, `/clock` is the punch surface, and there is no attendance-day screen at all. Consequences, all live: **overtime can never be approved** (so `pendingOtMinutes` can never become money — the corpus carries **469–513 hours per month**); a `needs_review` day is **excluded from payroll by `payableDays` and can never be resolved**, so it is never paid by anything; no day can be `verified`; no half day can be overridden. ⛔ **`/payroll` tells the manager *"approve it on the attendance day, then preview again"* (`apps/erp/src/app/payroll/page.tsx:217`) and there is no attendance day to approve it on.** ⚠️ **Root cause is a task-number collision:** T-154, T-156, T-157 and T-158 all name **T-166** as the review screen that would carry this; the inventory lane shipped **T-166 as *Deliveries and receiving***. The screen therefore has no number, no owner and no card — it was not descoped, it was overwritten | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **T-195, 2026-08-31.** `/attendance` (a queue that says WHY each day waits, above a roster) and `/attendance/[dayId]` (evidence read-only on the left, one control per adjustment kind on the right). ⭐ **The screen adds no gate of its own** — `recordAdjustment` already authorizes `manage_attendance`, refuses a frozen day and refuses self-adjudication; the page only *explains* a closed control. ⛔ **Building it immediately found DEF-115**, which is worse than the gap it was fixing. Closing tests: `attendance-review.test.ts`, 12 cases seeded from PUNCHES not days. Smoketested on 9008: the queue rendered T-194's held day with both its reasons, an overtime approval was recorded, and a **void moved a day to `voided`** — the first time either was possible from the running app. ⚠️ Phone-width rendering NOT visually confirmed: the browser harness would not resize the viewport. Desktop verified |
| **DEF-114** | 🔸 | ⚠️ **DEF-087's key counts contradict the raw capture that the golden test replays.** DEF-087 (captured 2026-08-29) states *"Lipa's `attendanceConfig` carries 22 keys; Malvar's carries 3 (`breakEnabled`, `enabledCredentials`, `shortShiftHalfDayOt`) and Banay-Banay's carries 1"*, and `tests/golden/attendance-adjudication.test.ts:55-59` repeats it. But `scripts/capture-attendance-golden.ts:84` reads the store document **raw, with no default merge**, and the fixture it produced on 2026-08-30 shows **Malvar with 23 keys and Banay-Banay with 21**, carrying store-specific non-default values — `allowedShiftStartHours: [10,12,17]` and `[9,14]` respectively, `latePolicy: record_only`, `lateGraceMinutes: 0`. Both stores are configured, not defaulted. Either DEF-087's probe mis-measured, or the two stores were configured between the 29th and the 30th | `reported` | ⚠️ **Re-probe before acting on either.** DEF-087's disposition assigns Ian an operational task — *"decide what Malvar and Banay-Banay's real hours and start times are"* — which may already be done. ⛔ Do not migrate either store's config until which reading is true is settled |
| **DEF-115** | ⛔ | ⭐ **VOIDING A DAY DID NOTHING, AND PAYROLL PAID IT IN FULL.** `recordAdjustment` accepts `kind: 'void'`, demands a reason, writes the audit row — and **nothing anywhere ever set `attendance_days.status`**. The day stayed `computed`, which is in `PAYABLE_DAY_STATUSES`, so `payableDays` picked it up and the pay run paid it. ⛔ Same hole in the other direction for `verify`: the sign-off was recorded, the status was never promoted, so **`verified` was unreachable** — and because `resolveStatus` also refuses to promote a `needs_review` day on recompute, **a flagged day could never be rescued by anything at all.** ⚠️ T-158 §7 states both promises in as many words — *"`verify` moves the day to `verified`"*, *"`void` moves it to `voided`. A voided day contributes nothing to payroll"* — and its invariant 11 is titled *"a voided day contributes nothing."* ⭐ **The cause is the test:** `resolveStatus` read `if (previous === 'voided' \|\| previous === 'verified') return previous`, which PRESERVES those statuses but can never PRODUCE one, and the invariant-11 test asserted only that the adjustment row was written and that the punches survived. **The half of the invariant that involved money lived in the test's name and not in its body.** ⛔ **Fourth occurrence of the DEF-109 family** (DEF-109, 111, 113, 115): a decision recorded in one place that the consuming layer never reflects | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **T-195, 2026-08-31.** The manager's decision becomes an **input** — `DayAdjustments.managerDecision`, populated by `toDayAdjustments` from the live `verify`/`void` row and honoured by `resolveStatus` — which is the architecture T-158 rests on everywhere else: *decisions as attributed inputs, never patches on an output.* ⭐ Strictly better than the sticky `previous` it replaces: superseding the decision now lets the day fall back to its computed status, where the old form could never be undone once set. A `void` outranks a `verify`. Closing tests: the two T-158 tests **strengthened to assert what their names promised**, plus *"the verification SURVIVES a later recompute"* |
| **DEF-116** | ⚠️ | ⭐ **THE T-166 COLLISION ORPHANED MORE THAN THE REVIEW SCREEN — but LESS than this row first claimed.** `docs/plans/phase-4-attendance.md:355` reads *"T-166 \| Missed clock-out sweep, on-shift roster, current-day punch feed (hot-set item 4)"* — three deliverables bundled under the number the inventory lane took for Deliveries. ⛔ **CORRECTED 2026-08-31, same day: this row originally said the roster and the feed were "discussed nowhere". That was wrong, and checking beat asserting.** The truth, per grep and a live probe: **(1) the current-day punch feed IS BUILT** — T-181's migration `0088` creates an `attendance_days` trigger enqueueing `day_feed`, `MIRROR_ENTITIES` carries it, and `publisher.ts:214` resolves it to `mirror/{storeId}/dayFeed/{dayId}` with staff code and display name. **(2) The missed clock-out sweep is genuinely absent** — no cron, no job, and v2 has **no scheduled-job surface at all** (`apps/erp/src/app/api/` contains only `health`), so building it means building that surface first. **(3) The on-shift roster** has no ERP screen; the mirror arguably serves the terminal's need already, since a day with status `open` IS on-shift | ⚠️ **PARTLY CLOSED** | ✅ **The roster and the punch feed are closed by T-200, 2026-08-31** — the clock terminal now renders both, so hot-set item 4 is read for the first time since T-181 published it. ⛔ **The missed clock-out sweep remains open and is specced as T-199**, not built: it needs `auto_clock_out_hour` and `max_shift_hours` columns v2 does not have, and it carries DEF-119 with it. ⚠️ Its blocker is not code — v2 has **no scheduled-job surface installed**; T-140 decided the pattern (a CLI job on the host's timer) and **nobody has installed a timer on the droplet**, which is the step that failed for v1's depreciation and twice on POS |
| **DEF-117** | ⚠️ | ⭐ **BIBLE ROW 122'S SIGNAL HAS NO READER: NOTHING SURFACES OR BLOCKS ON UNVERIFIED SOURCE DOCUMENTS.** Row 122 decided that *"capture is always allowed; VERIFICATION is what is withheld"* and rested on the consequence being visible — *"a growing pile of unpostable drafts is a more legible signal than a greyed-out button, and it is visible to the admin who can act."* **Nothing makes it visible.** `listFindings` (`packages/domain/src/findings/index.ts:56-78`) runs twelve detectors — trial-balance drift, reciprocal drift, unpartied control lines, opening residual, clearing not at rest, missed periodic postings, unremitted ceiling, remittance overdue, service-charge float ageing, till float off target, till count variance, items missing threshold — and **not one of them counts a `pending` supplier invoice or an unposted expense.** `closeReadiness` (`:154-165`) filters that same feed for `severity === 'hard'`, so a period can be reported ready to close while invoices sit unverified and their expense never enters the period at all. ⚠️ **This is live TODAY, before T-196** — a `pending` invoice already holds an unrecorded expense out of its period indefinitely, and the close cannot see it. T-196 (drafts against pending invoices) does not create the hole; it **raises the stakes**, because after it the pile is real rows with real amounts rather than an absence. ⭐ **The fix is one detector, not a design** — pending invoices and draft expenses whose `business_date` falls in the closing period, `hard` if the period is being closed and `soft` otherwise — but it is a cross-module call about close policy, so it is logged rather than folded into T-196 (whose Out of scope names it). ⭐ **The likely first consumer is T-193 (statutory remittance), not this task** — checked with the attendance/payroll lane 2026-08-31: nothing shipped there today depends on the hook (payroll posts through the ordinary path and the freeze is on `attendance_days`, not the period), but a module that **settles a liability** is exactly the one that wants a close-blocking signal. Whoever builds T-193 should expect to need this detector and should not assume it exists. | `reported` — verified by reading `findings/index.ts`; no test written | **Needs a task.** Not T-196's to fix — that spec deliberately excludes it |
| **DEF-118** | ⚠️ | ⭐ **THE CLOCK-TERMINAL MIRROR PUBLISHES EVERY ATTENDANCE DAY EVER, NOT THE CURRENT ONE.** Bible §5.3 defines hot-set item 4 as *"On-shift roster + **current business day** punch feed"* — *small*, refreshed *every few min*. But `mirror_enqueue` (migration `0088`, line 54) takes no date filter and the trigger fires `after insert or update on attendance_days` **for every row**, so any touch of a historical day republishes it to the terminal mirror. ⛔ **Measured on the dev database: 24 of 24 `day_feed` publishes are for days that are NOT the current business day — 100%.** ⚠️ **And payroll doubles it:** T-189's `freezeDays` updates every day a run pays, so posting a two-week run for one person enqueued a second publish for each of ten historical days. At three stores and ~40 staff a semi-monthly run would enqueue **hundreds of mirror writes for days no terminal will ever display**. ⭐ Bible §5.3's hot set is a **closed list** whose whole point is that the edge holds the minimum; this quietly widened it to the entire attendance history | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **T-198, 2026-08-31.** Migration `0112` recreates the trigger with `when (new.shift_day >= ((now() at time zone 'Asia/Manila')::date - 1))`. ⭐ **A trigger `WHEN`, not a publisher filter** — the cost is the outbox row *and* the Firestore write, both upstream of anything the publisher could decide. ⚠️ **Two days, and the second is not slack**: a night shift beginning 22:00 belongs to the previous business day, and offline replay — the entire point of the inbox lane — arrives hours after the fact on the same `shift_day`. ⚠️ `(now() at time zone 'Asia/Manila')::date`, never bare `current_date`, which follows the SESSION timezone and would roll the window at 08:00 local on a UTC server. Closing tests: 5 in `mirror-publisher.test.ts`, and **verified by reverting the trigger on the test database first** — the two "must not publish" cases failed with `expected 1 to be +0` and the three "must publish" cases still passed. ⛔ **Not fixed, and named in T-198 §2.2:** documents already pushed to Firestore stay there, so **T-200 must query `dayFeed` by `shiftDay` rather than subscribing to the whole collection** |
| **DEF-121** | ⚠️ | ⭐ **`drizzle-kit generate` HAS BEEN EFFECTIVELY UNUSABLE SINCE ~MIGRATION 0020, AND NOBODY HAD NOTICED.** `packages/db/migrations/meta/` holds snapshot files for only 12 of 115 migrations (`0000`–`0019`, gaps included; nothing from `0020` through `0114`). Found while adding T-191's two tables: `db:generate` opened by asking whether `expenses.supplier_invoice_id` — created in migration `0043`, ~72 migrations ago — was a create or a rename from `receipt_photo_url`, a column that has not existed in `schema/expenses.ts` since T-130. `_journal.json` is intact and correctly ordered (95 migrations applied fine through it), so nothing is actually broken **for applying migrations** — `migrate.ts` reads the journal and runs raw SQL, never touching snapshots. What is broken is `drizzle-kit`'s own diffing, which falls back to comparing current `schema.ts` against its last real snapshot (`0019`) and re-litigates every rename since. ⚠️ **Every migration since roughly 0020 was therefore hand-written from `schema.ts`, not generated** — consistent with the "hand-written per DEF-073" note already on most of them, but the REASON was assumed to be "Drizzle can't express a GiST exclude / IMMUTABLE function," not "the tool cannot run cleanly at all." **Not independently verified**: whether the gap started exactly at 0020 or snapshots were deliberately pruned at some point is unconfirmed — stated as reported per §13 row 164 | `closed 2026-09-10 → DEF-242 / ADR-012 / bible row 191` — originally `reported`, found 2026-09-01 while generating T-191's migration; worked around by hand-writing `0115` directly rather than answering the prompt | Low urgency: hand-writing migrations already IS this project's practice, so the tool being unusable changes nothing about the day-to-day. **Worth a deliberate fix eventually** — either regenerate the missing snapshots from `schema.ts` as of each historical migration (expensive, error-prone, probably not worth it) or accept hand-writing as the permanent house style and say so explicitly in `docs/RULES.md` rather than leaving the next session to discover this the same way. ⭐ **Closed by DEF-242 → ADR-012 (2026-09-10):** the second option, with the first measured and rejected on evidence; RULES rule 9 now says it |
| **DEF-122** | 🔸 | **`docs/tasks/130-accounts-payable.md`'s status line read "Spec ready, not yet implemented" for days after T-130 actually shipped (`f438316`, 2026-08-28).** Found 2026-09-02 while assessing T-170 (supplier returns), which needed to know whether to extend `recordPayment`'s allocation or build a parallel one — the spec's own §5 says "if T-130 is built before this task starts, that is the better order… report which way it went," and the status line was the thing that would answer that, wrongly. `packages/domain/src/ap/index.ts` is 874 lines, fully implemented, and its own header even documents a LATER task (T-196) correcting it — so the drift was not a same-day oversight, it sat stale across multiple sessions' worth of work landing on top of it. ⚠️ **No later task's spec cross-referenced T-130 enough to notice** — T-131 (AR, built same-day as its own spec) and T-179 (customer returns) both depend on AP/AR patterns without needing to read T-130's status line directly | `fixed` | Status line corrected 2026-09-02, same session as the finding, naming the commit and what future readers (T-170 first) need from it. No code changed — this is a documentation-drift defect, not a behavioural one. **Worth naming the general pattern**: a task spec's status line is written once at commit time and has no forcing function to update later — `wrap-up`'s roadmap refresh catches `docs/ROADMAP.html` drift but not individual task-spec status lines, which is exactly how this one persisted unnoticed |
| **DEF-123** | 🔸 | **`money-transfers` (T-139, shipped 2026-08-2x) let a user select the SAME account as both "From" and "To"** — the domain layer already refuses it (`packages/domain/src/money-transfers/index.ts:103`), but the picker offered the mistake anyway and let it round-trip to a server error before saying so. Found 2026-09-02 by Ian, live-testing T-167's new stock-transfers screen, which shipped with the identical gap (two independent `<select>`s over one room list, no exclusion) — he flagged it on the NEW screen, and the same shape turned out to already be live on `money-transfers`. ⭐ **Not treated as two one-off fixes**: Ian's own framing was *"this must be central logic to all transfers including money in accounts,"* so the fix is one shared component (`apps/erp/src/components/two-way-picker.tsx`), not two ad hoc patches | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **Same session, 2026-09-02.** `TwoWayPicker` filters the "To" list to exclude whatever "From" currently is — deliberately ONE-DIRECTIONAL, not both ways. ⛔ **A bidirectional version was tried first and had a real deadlock**: with exactly two options (T-167's common case — a store's first two rooms), each list excluding the other's current value left both fields with only their own value selectable, so direction could never be reversed through the dropdowns at all. `money-transfers/page.tsx`'s `AccountPicker`/`Field` and `RecordTransferForm`'s inline form were both replaced by the shared component; `stock-transfers`'s `NewTransferForm` was built on it from the start once the bug was found. ⚠️ **A plain formatter function (`peso`) cannot cross the server→client boundary as a prop** — caught by Next.js itself at runtime (`Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components`), fixed by building the account labels server-side in `page.tsx` and passing finished strings, not the formatter |
| **DEF-124** | 🔸 | **Nothing ever sets `stock_layers.invoice_line_id` after a layer is created, so `is_provisional` is permanently `true` for every layer in the system, regardless of whether its receipt's supplier invoice has since arrived.** Found 2026-09-02 while building T-170's `credit_basis` derivation, which needs to read a layer's invoiced status. `packages/domain/src/stock/invoice-link.ts`'s `linkInvoiceToReceipt` (T-163's second posting) writes only the JOURNAL entry when an invoice arrives — `Dr 2120 / Cr 2110`, re-costing the open share — and never touches `stock_layers` at all. `createLayer` sets `invoiceLineId` once, at receipt time, always `null`. Grepped: no caller anywhere writes to `stock_layers.invoice_line_id` after creation. ⚠️ **Also structural**: `supplier_invoice_lines` carries no `delivery_line_id` or `layer_id` column, so even a future writer has nothing to join a specific invoice line back to a specific layer — the schema doesn't currently support the link `is_provisional`'s own doc comment describes ("false exactly when `invoice_line_id` is present"). ⭐ Not a T-170 defect — T-170's own derivation is correct against what the flag says; this is what the flag currently always says | `reported` — found in passing, not investigated further; not this session's task | Low urgency today: nothing yet reads `is_provisional` for a business decision except T-170's `credit_basis` (which safely defaults toward the more generous, spendable-credit basis when a layer reads as provisional). Becomes load-bearing the moment anything relies on it meaning "this receipt's invoice arrived" — worth a real fix (either `linkInvoiceToReceipt` updates the receipt's own layers directly, since it already has `receiptId`, or the per-layer flag is reconsidered as the wrong grain and the confirmation status moves to the receipt/delivery instead, matching `docs/tasks/163-receipt-posts-value.md`'s own header note about a `cost-confirmation.ts` module that does not appear to exist in the tree) |

Full suite: 1861/1861 (2 apparent failures were parallel-worker DB deadlocks on an unrelated file, confirmed by isolated re-run). ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT done in this pass, named rather than silently skipped**: T-161 invariant 9's `journal_entry_id` CHECK tightening (Scope's migration item) — no caller anywhere, including this rewiring, currently backfills a movement's `journal_entry_id` after its entry posts, so the constraint would break every existing consumption path, not just this one; a real fix touches `usage.ts`/`consumption-posting.ts` too and needs its own pass. **Also found and fixed in the same pass**: DEF-126 (a `supplierCreditAgeing` uuid/text join bug from T-170, caught only by running the full suite) and DEF-127 (T-151 outbound freight has nowhere to post under the new shape — reported, not invented a fix for). `inventoryControlDoesNotMatchValuation` registered as a live finding (Phase 3's own exit gate, org-wide). ⛔ **Not smoketested live on 9008** — no `apps/erp` screen exists for trade orders at all; this is domain + test coverage only, same limitation the defect itself named | ⭐ **T-165 (THE PERIODIC → PERPETUAL SWITCH) IS MARKED DONE IN `ROADMAP.html` AND IS NOT BUILT — TRADE-ORDER DISPATCH STILL POSTS NO COGS AND MOVES NO REAL INVENTORY.** Found 2026-09-02 starting T-179 (customer returns), whose entire external-return path (§5: *"the return is not a new mechanic — it is `reverseConsumption` on the sale's movements"*) assumes a wholesale dispatch already created a `stock_layer_consumptions` row to reverse. **It does not.** `packages/domain/src/trade/index.ts`'s `dispatchTradeOrder` — the ONLY dispatch function anything calls (grepped: no `apps/erp` screen exists for trade orders at all yet, so this is exercised only by integration tests) — has two branches, and NEITHER touches stock: the `external` branch calls only `recordCharge` (`Dr 1210 / Cr 4300`, no COGS, no `consume()`); the `internal` branch still posts the OLD periodic four-leg entry (`5175`/`1250`/`5170`/`2150`) instead of calling `commissary/transfer.ts`'s `dispatchOrder`/`confirmReceipt` (T-172/173, 20 green tests, real `1310`/`1330`/`1340` movements) — which `grep`-confirmed is still "called by nothing," exactly as that file's own header says. **T-165's own spec names this precisely**: its §4 "UPDATE 2026-08-29" describes the rewiring as three unstarted steps (point the internal branch at `dispatchOrder`, wire a `confirmReceipt` caller, delete the `5170`/`5175` legs), and its **Done-when checklist has one of eight items checked** — only the bible amendment (ADR-011 rows 165–166). **Independently confirmed a third way**: bible §16.7's own closing criteria for this task (defects #5 "no inventory valuation" and #6 "no period-lock guard") carry no closure marker. ⚠️ **`ROADMAP.html`'s note text for T-165 — "A dated split, per ADR-011" — describes a design the spec explicitly REJECTED** (§5: *"There is no boundary — decided, and the absence is the design… do not add a date branch 'just in case.'"*), so the roadmap line was never updated past an early draft of the ADR-011 decision. ⛔ **Consequence, live today**: every wholesale/franchise sale currently overstates gross margin (COGS is silently zero); every internal branch↔commissary trade order silently fails to move real stock at either store under the perpetual model Phase 3 otherwise enforces everywhere else (deliveries, usage, transfers, returns all correctly touch `stock_layers`/`stock_movements` — trade orders are the one path that does not) — which directly compromises Phase 3's own exit gate (`Σ 1310–1340 = Σ open-layer value`) the moment a trade order dispatches. **T-165's own header calls this "the point of Wave 0"** and states Wave 0 must ship atomically ("T-160–T-165 ship as one deploy, or none of them do") — that rule was violated: T-160–164 shipped and were marked done while T-165's actual rewiring did not | `reported` — verified by reading `trade/index.ts`, `commissary/transfer.ts`, T-165's own Done-when checklist, and bible §16.7; | **DEF-128** | ⛔ | ⭐ **`stockLayersRepo.reverseConsumption` IGNORED A PARTIAL `give` AND ALWAYS REVERSED A CONSUMPTION ROW'S FULL QUANTITY — SILENTLY WRONG THE MOMENT TWO PARTIAL REVERSALS CROSSED ONE ROW.** T-162's own `planReversal` correctly computes a PARTIAL take from a row when the requested reversal quantity falls short of that row's full amount (`give = min(line.quantity, outstanding)`) — but the write-side `reverseConsumption(tx, consumptionId, reversedByMovementId)` took no quantity parameter at all: it always credited the row's FULL `quantity` back to the layer and marked the WHOLE row `reversedByMovementId`, regardless of what the plan actually asked for. Found 2026-09-02 building T-179's test 3 (a partial return spanning two consumed layers) — the second of two sequential partial returns failed with `"only 5.000 was consumed, 6.000 requested"`, because the first return's nominal 2kg take from a 3kg row had silently reversed the WHOLE 3kg. ⚠️ **Dormant until now**: every existing caller (T-164's `voidUsageRecord`, T-170's `voidReturn`, T-165's void) happened to always reverse a FULL original movement in one call, never a plan that stopped short partway through a row — a partial reversal genuinely spanning row boundaries is exactly the case T-179 introduces for the first time. | ✅ **FIXED, same session** | ✅ `reverseConsumption` now takes `quantity` and, when it is LESS than the row's own amount, SPLITS: the original row shrinks to what remains outstanding (its `unit_cost` untouched — invariant 5's fact stays fixed), and a NEW row, already reversed, carries exactly what was given back, against the SAME original movement. Required a migration (`0120`) relaxing `stock_layer_consumptions_layer_movement_key` to a PARTIAL unique index (`WHERE reversed_by_movement_id IS NULL`) — the old plain unique index forbade two rows for one (layer, movement) pair outright, which a legitimate split needs. `consume.ts`'s `reverseConsumption` (the one caller) passes `line.quantity` through. Regression-verified: `stock-usage.test.ts`, `supplier-returns.test.ts`, `trade.test.ts`, `commissary-transfer.test.ts`, `count-sessions.test.ts` — 113/113 still green |

readKeyFile(file)`, and `??` does not treat `''` as absent, so `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY=` set-but-blank (this repo's normal shape, `_FILE` being the "preferred" one) fed `JSON.parse('')` and threw "did not parse as JSON" on **every** real call — never caught before because nothing had ever called either factory outside a test, which is this very defect. | **DEF-134** | ⛔ | ⭐ **NO LIVE PATH COMPUTES AN `attendance_days` ROW FOR THE FIRST TIME FROM FRESH PUNCHES — T-157's OWN SPEC CLAIMS CALLERS THAT DO NOT EXIST.** Found 2026-09-02, same audit. T-157 (Adjudication) is marked `t-done`, and its own spec text asserts *"the ingestor, the manager screen, the payroll preview, the roster — [all] call `adjudicateDay`."* None of the three do: `packages/domain/src/sync/ingestor.ts` never references `adjudicateDay`/`adjudicateRange`; `packages/domain/src/attendance/review.ts`'s `attendanceReviewQueue`/`attendanceRoster`/`attendanceDayDetail` are pure reads; `apps/erp/src/app/payroll/lib.ts` never calls it either. `upsertDay` (the function that CREATES an `attendance_days` row) is called only from inside `adjudicateDay` itself, and `adjudicateDay`'s only real caller is `recordAdjustment` — reachable only via a UI route that requires a **pre-existing** `dayId` | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **T-204, 2026-09-04, same fix as DEF-132.** `ingestOne` (`packages/domain/src/sync/ingestor.ts`) now calls `adjudicateDay` immediately after a genuinely NEW `recordPunch` — gated on `!alreadyProcessed`, so a replay never re-triggers it. The call runs inside its OWN nested savepoint (`tx.transaction((inner) => adjudicateDay(inner as Tx, {...}))`): Postgres aborts the whole enclosing transaction on the first failing statement, and `adjudicateDay` throws on an already-frozen day, so without the savepoint a replayed old punch landing on a closed pay-period day would have un-written the punch `recordPunch` had just correctly recorded a moment earlier in the SAME transaction. `IngestReport` gains `adjudicated`/`adjudicationFailed`/`adjudicationFailures` (additive — every existing report-shape assertion, including T-182 invariant 10's "the numbers add up to `seen`", stays true for the original fields). Closing tests (new, `inbox-ingestor.test.ts`): a new punch produces an `attendance_days` row that did not exist before · a replay does NOT call `adjudicateDay` again (asserted via the report's `adjudicated` count and a stable day-row id) · a punch for an already-frozen day (a real `pay_runs` row, FK-backed) still records the punch, is never quarantined, and reports the adjudication failure by name — the day itself is left untouched. ⚠️ **One pre-existing T-182 stress test needed a longer timeout, not a different assertion**: "a batch of 500 where 499 are replays" ingests 499 GENUINELY NEW punches on its first call, so each one now also adjudicates — 499 sequential recomputes exceed the default 30s budget even though every step is correct; bumped to 180s (isolated run: ~28s). `adjudicateDay`/`compute-day.ts` are unmodified, untouched, exactly as this task's Scope required. Worth knowing for production sizing, not treated as a defect: a relay batch of genuinely-new punches (`limit: 500`) could now legitimately take tens of seconds, which the 2-minute timer cadence has headroom for in the ordinary case |
