T-132 (wholesale orders) and T-133 (inter-entity trade) are both marked `t-done`. `createTradeOrder`/`dispatchTradeOrder`/`receiveTradeOrder` (`packages/domain/src/trade/index.ts`) — DEF-125 confirmed their INTERNAL posting logic is now genuinely correct — have **zero callers anywhere in `apps/`**. `nav.tsx`'s Trade group links Suppliers, Customers, Price lists, and Customer returns, but no trade-order screen at all | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **2026-09-03.** `apps/erp/src/app/trade/orders/` — a register (`/trade/orders`, per store, listing every order the store is on from EITHER end, the same both-directions rule as `/commissary/orders`), a create form (`NewOrderForm.tsx`, client: dynamic line rows, the rate OPTIONAL and its placeholder showing what `createTradeOrder` will default to — the customer's price-list rate, or the branch price for an internal counterparty — so the agreed price is seen before it is captured), and a detail page (`/trade/orders/[id]`) with **Dispatch** (credit-override reason input when the customer has a limit), **Receive** (per-line signed-for quantity + shortage reason for an internal transfer, T-174's short-receipt input; a plain signature for a sale) and **Void** (not offered after an internal receipt, which the domain refuses — T-165's stated limit). Actions run under `withOrgPrincipal` like AP's; errors land as banners via the customers screen's redirect pattern rather than the returns screen's thrown error. `nav.tsx` Trade group gains **Trade orders**. Two repo reads added, no domain change: `tradeRepo.listOrders` and `itemsRepo.listItemPricing` (one query for the handful of ruled items, instead of `branchPriceFor` per catalogue item); `TradeOrderRow` widened to expose fees/void/requested-by columns the `select()` already returned. **Smoketested live on 9008 through the real screens** (scripted headless Chromium, 2026-09-03, only `commissary_item_map` seeded by SQL — DEF-129): price list + ₱180/kg rate → external + internal customers → a wholesale line on a non-wholesale item **refused with the domain's own message on screen** → order priced off the list with the rate left blank (₱360.00 captured) → dispatch → DB shows `1210`/`4300` ₱360 and `5100`/`1310` ₱180 COGS at FIFO cost, one `usage` movement → receive records the signature only → internal transfer 3 kg @ ₱350 → dispatch shows the ₱270 dispatch cost, `1340`/`1310` at cost → **short receipt, 2 of 3, damaged in transit** → `1250`/`2150` ₱700 with each party the OTHER store's code, branch `1310` ₱700, `1340` relieved ₱180, `5175` ₱520 margin, `transfer_in` 2 kg at Lipa, one open `trade_discrepancies` row (1 kg, ₱90) → Lipa's register shows the transfer and not the sale → a draft voided with no reversal. +2 integration tests in `trade.test.ts` for the two reads. ⚠️ **Left as they were, named rather than hidden**: the branch-price default is exercised by the existing test only (no `setItemPricing` screen exists to set a rule live); `commissary_item_map` still has no writer (DEF-129) so a real internal receipt still needs SQL first; the `confirmed` state has no button (dispatch accepts a draft directly, and nothing in the domain distinguishes the two) |
| **DEF-139** | 🔸 | **The senior/PWD statutory discount register screen exists and is genuinely wired to real server actions, but is linked from nowhere — not `nav.tsx`, not any other page.** Found 2026-09-02, same audit. T-137 is marked `t-done`. `/statutory-discounts` (`apps/erp/src/app/statutory-discounts/`) has real actions and reads real data, but `nav.tsx` has no matching entry and no other page links to it. ⚠️ **`nav.tsx`'s own header comment describes exactly this failure mode as the reason it was built** — a prior defect where routes existed and were linked from nowhere, invisible in both a test suite and a build — and this screen slipped through that same fix | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **2026-09-04.** `nav.tsx`'s Reports group gains `{ href: '/statutory-discounts', label: 'SC/PWD register' }`, placed beside Trial balance/Income statement rather than Daily's collection screens since this is a compliance register, not a money-clearing action. One-line fix as scoped — no code beyond the nav array touched. Not live-smoketested beyond confirming the link renders and resolves; the screen's own actions were already verified wired in this row's original finding |
| **DEF-140** | ⛔ | ⭐ **A BRANCH RECEIPT OF TWO COMMISSARY LINES THAT MAP TO ONE BRANCH ITEM THREW, AND THE WHOLE RECEIPT ROLLED BACK.** `confirmReceipt` (`packages/domain/src/commissary/transfer.ts`, T-173) wrote every branch `stock_movements` row with `source_id = orderId`; `stock_movements_source_pair_key` (T-161, migration `0063`) allows one non-reversed movement per (source, item, location). Two order lines whose `commissary_item_map` rows point at the SAME branch item — the everyday case of a slab and its trimmed cut both arriving as "pork belly", and exactly what T-171 invariant 4's per-(commissary, branch, item) map permits — therefore produced two movements with one source pair, and Postgres refused the second: `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "stock_movements_source_pair_key"`. The dispatch had already posted, so the goods sat in `1340` with a receipt that could not be confirmed. Found 2026-09-03 by the T-172 live smoketest on 9008 — the first receipt ever made through the screens with two lines — after every single-line test since T-173 had passed | ✅ **FIXED, same session** | ✅ The branch movement's `source_id` is now `${orderId}:${lineId}` — the source of a receipt movement is the LINE, which is what the index is for: a retried receipt of the same line still writes nothing new. The layer keeps the order as its source and the movement as its key; nothing reads either back by id (grepped). Closing test: `branch-orders.test.ts` *"DEF-140: two commissary items mapped to ONE branch item are both received"* — asserts two movements and two layers at two rates, never one merged layer at a made-up cost. Re-verified live: the same stuck order received on the second attempt. ⚠️ Lesson, DEF-126's shape again: a file's own 20 tests all used one item at both ends, so the map's many-to-one direction was never exercised until a screen made it easy to do |
| **DEF-141** | ⛔ | ⭐ **VOIDING A POSTED USAGE OR WASTAGE RECORD PUT THE STOCK AND THE LAYERS BACK AND LEFT THE `Dr 5110/5300 · Cr 1310` ENTRY STANDING — A VOIDED USAGE SILENTLY KEPT ITS COGS.** T-168's invariant 7 says *"void reverses stock, layers and entry in one transaction"* and its test 8 says *"void reverses stock and entry"*; `packages/domain/src/stock/usage.ts`'s `voidUsageRecord` called only `reverseConsumption` (stock + layers), and the engine's own test (`stock-usage.test.ts` *"restores each layer exactly"*) asserted the layers and on-hand and never a balance — so the missing third was invisible to a green suite. Nothing stored the entry ids on the record or its lines, and the entries are posted one per (inventory account, COGS category) group under `source_id = '<recordId>:<account>:<category>'`, so a caller had nothing to hand `reverseEntry` even if it knew to. Found 2026-09-03 by the T-168 lane wiring the first real `voidUsage` document function (DEF-135) — the exact shape DEF-135 warned of: an engine green for five days with no caller to notice | ✅ **FIXED, same session** | ✅ `journalRepo.findPostedEntriesBySource(tx, orgId, sourceType, sourceIdPrefix)` — the `(source_type, source_id)` pair the journal already indexes, filtered to `posted` originals (a reversal carries the SAME source pair as its original, so a naive match would hand a second void the first void's entries). `voidUsageRecord` now reverses every entry it finds through `reverseEntry` — the ledger's own discipline (idempotent by `reverse:{entryId}`, linked, original flipped to `reversed`), dated `voidedOn`, memo = the caller's `reason` (a new optional `VoidUsageInput.reason`, defaulting to naming the record) — AFTER the stock and layers and inside the same transaction, so a closed-period refusal on the reversal unwinds the stock with it. Closing test: `stock-usage.test.ts` *"and REVERSES the entry — 5110 and 1310 return to their pre-post balances"*: `5300` back to 0.00, `1310` back to 1,960.00, original `reversed`, reversal points at it, memo and date as given. 20/20 on the file. ⚠️ Lesson, DEF-126/140's shape again: the test asserted the mechanism it was written to prove (per-layer restoration) and nothing else, so the invariant's third clause had no assertion anywhere |
| **DEF-142** | ⛔ | ⭐ **THE COUNT FREEZE REFUSED THE COUNT'S OWN RESOLVING WRITE — A REAL COUNT COULD NEVER COMMIT.** `stock_movements_check_not_frozen_trg` (T-169, migration `0068`) refuses ANY insert into a room an active session holds. Every counted line's room is, by construction, one of the session's own frozen rooms, and `commitCountSession` writes its `count_adjustment` movement into exactly that room — so the freeze a session created blocked its own resolution. ⚠️ **The engine's own tests dodged it and said so**: every commit case in `count-sessions.test.ts` froze the stockroom while counting the kitchen — *"freeze a different room so the write can land"* — a shape no real count has. Found 2026-09-03 by the T-169 lane wiring the first real `commitCount` workflow (DEF-135); the lane worked around it by flipping the session to `closed` before calling the engine, in the same transaction | ✅ **FIXED at the root, same session** | ✅ Migration `0123_def142_count_freeze_exempts_own_commit` replaces the trigger function with ONE exemption: a `count_adjustment` movement with `source_type = 'count'` whose `source_id` names a session that holds THIS room passes; everything else into a frozen room stays refused, including a count movement from a DIFFERENT session (pinned by a new engine test). The engine tests now freeze the SAME room they count. `count-workflow.ts`'s `commitCount` returned to the natural order — engine first, then the lines' audit trail, then `markCountCommitted` closes the session — so a business refusal from the engine (counted long with no cost basis, an uncounted item) is handed back as `{ ok: false }` with nothing written, and the lane's `releaseFreezeForCommit` pre-flip is gone. ⚠️ The same-day review then caught the exemption not checking the naming session was still ACTIVE — a closed session's id would have opened the freeze a later session placed on the same room; `0123` was amended before commit (local-only, T-160 §9.3's precedent) to join `count_sessions` and require an active status, re-applied by hand to the three databases that had it, and a third engine test pins a stale id being refused. ⚠️ Lesson, DEF-126/140/141's shape a fourth time in one day: a test that arranges its fixture so the code under test can pass is a test of the fixture |
| **DEF-143** | ⛔ | ⭐ **A MIXED-KIND COUNT POSTED ITS WHOLE VARIANCE AGAINST ONE INVENTORY ACCOUNT.** `commitCountSession` (T-169's engine, `packages/domain/src/stock/count-sessions.ts`) kept `inventoryAccount` as a single variable reassigned on every varying line and used it for the one short entry and the one long entry — so a commissary counting pork (`1310`) and takeout boxes (`1320`) short in one session credited BOTH losses to whichever account the last varying line carried. `1310` and `1320` drift in opposite directions and the inventory control accounts stop tying to the layers (bible invariant 6). Dormant while every count test used one item; reachable the moment T-169's screen let one session hold two kinds. Found by the 2026-09-03 Opus review of DEF-135's lanes | ✅ **FIXED, same day** | ✅ Cost lines are grouped by each line's OWN inventory account and one entry is posted per (account, direction) — `CommitCountResult` gains `shortEntryIds`/`longEntryIds`, `shortEntryId`/`longEntryId` stay as the first of each for callers that store one. Closing test: `count-sessions.test.ts` *"a MIXED-kind count posts each inventory account its own items' cost"* — pork short 2 kg and boxes short 4 → `1310` −₱200, `1320` −₱20, `5400` +₱220, two short entries |
| **DEF-144** | ⛔ | ⭐ **THREE MULTI-LINE ENGINE LOOPS RETURNED A REFUSAL AFTER AN EARLIER LINE HAD ALREADY WRITTEN — AND THE CALLER COMMITTED THE HALF-POST.** `postUsageRecord` and `voidUsageRecord` (`usage.ts`) and `commitCountSession`'s short path (`count-sessions.ts`) each looped `consume()`/`reverseConsumption()` with `if (!result.ok) return result` — correct for line 1, wrong for line 2+, where line 1's movement and `stock_layer_consumptions` rows are already written. `postUsage` passed the `ok:false` up and `usage/actions.ts` returned it out of `withOrgPrincipal`, which COMMITS. Scenario: a two-line draft; between draft and post another writer depletes item B's layers (exactly the drift T-168 §12.6 saw live) → post → item A consumed, no entry, record still `draft`, and a second press consumes A again. `usage.ts` already stated the rule for its posting step ("stock is already written — throw") and did not apply it to its own loop. Found by the 2026-09-03 review | ✅ **FIXED, same day** | ✅ All three loops throw once anything has been written (`refused after stock was written` / `after stock was reversed`), so the transaction unwinds; a refusal on the FIRST line is still returned as `{ ok: false }` with nothing to unwind. Closing test: `stock-usage.test.ts` *"a refusal on line 2 UNWINDS line 1"* — 5 kg of pork then 1 kg of an item with no stock → throws, on-hand unchanged, zero consumptions, no `5110` |
| **DEF-119** | ⛔ | ⭐ **`halfDayOnMissingTap` IS A COLUMN NOTHING READS, AND v2 THEREFORE PAYS ₱0 WHERE v1 PAYS HALF A DAY.** `store_attendance_config.half_day_on_missing_tap` exists in the schema (T-155, migration `0081`) and `grep -rn halfDayOnMissingTap packages/domain/src/` returns **nothing** — no reader, in `compute-day.ts` or anywhere else. ⚠️ **All three operating stores have it ON in live v1**, verified by `ianla-ce` reading the production store documents on 2026-08-31, alongside `autoClockOutHour: 2` at each. In v1 it is what decides what a forgotten tap-out pays. In v2 a day with an `in` and no `out` is `open`, credits **zero minutes**, and sits there for ever — so a staffer who forgets to tap out is paid **nothing** where v1 would pay them **half a day**. ⛔ **This one costs STAFF money**, which is the opposite direction to DEF-111 and arguably worse: an underpayment nobody will report because nobody can see it. ⭐ **Fifth occurrence of the DEF-109 family** (109, 111, 113, 115, 119) — stored intent that the consuming layer never reads. ⚠️ v2 also lacks the two columns the rule needs to fire on a schedule at all: **no `auto_clock_out_hour`, no `max_shift_hours`** | ✅ **FIXED** | ✅ **T-199, 2026-08-31.** `computeDay` reads the flag: a day with an `in` and no `out`, at a store that opted in, credits `halfDayMinutes` and sets `isHalfDay`. ⭐ **And no end time is invented to get there** — v1's rule is *"the staffer is clocked out, but the time is not captured; nobody observed them leaving"*, so the half day is a CREDIT, not a reconstructed shift. `endMinute` stays null and a test asserts it. ⚠️ A missing **in** is deliberately NOT credited whatever the flag says: there is no anchor, so nothing to credit from and no evidence anybody arrived when they say they did. Migration `0114` adds the two columns the sweep needs (`auto_clock_out_hour` nullable, `max_shift_hours` default 16) |

| **DEF-145** | ⛔ | ⭐ **A LOAN REPAID OUT OF A PLATFORM PAYOUT WAS INVISIBLE TO THE LOAN — THE LEDGER WAS RIGHT AND THE LOAN SCREEN SHOWED THE DEBT UNPAID.** `positionOf` and `positionsByLoan` (`packages/db/src/repo/loans.ts`) filtered `je.source_type in ('loan_disbursement','loan_payment')`, which assumes the loan module is the only thing that ever moves `2400`. It is not: T-150 §6 posts the platform's dock as a **leg inside the `channel_settlement` entry** — deliberately, so a void unwinds both in one transaction — and **two of v1's live channels do exactly this, one of them cross-store.** So a ₱2,000 repayment left `2400` correct, the trial balance correct, and `/loans` reporting the full amount still outstanding, with nothing anywhere disagreeing loudly enough to notice. ⚠️ The engine's own test asserted the `2400` **leg existed** and never that the loan's **position moved** — DEF-141/142's shape a fifth time. Found 2026-09-03 wiring DEF-137's first real caller | ✅ **FIXED, same session** | ✅ Both readers now key on the **account and party** — `2400` is party-requiring and the party is the loan (bible row 158) — so any non-disbursement movement counts, including repayment paths nobody has invented yet. This is bible row 145's rule in a second place: *“summed over enumerated document types, any transaction missing from the enumeration silently breaks the reconciliation.”* `positionsByLoan` becomes a union because `7200` carries no party. Closing tests: `channel-settlements.test.ts` *“DEF-145 — a loan docked out of a payout MOVES THE LOAN'S POSITION”* (**confirmed reading `0` against `HEAD` before the fix**, then 2,000 / 3,000 after, with `positionsByLoan` asserted to agree) and *“voiding that settlement puts the LOAN back too”* (5,000 outstanding, 0 paid). ⚠️ **Named and NOT fixed: the settlement books the whole dock to PRINCIPAL and posts no interest**, where bible row 158 says the split is computed. `paymentsFor` and `lastPaymentDate` are also left narrow on purpose — a settlement is not a loan-payment document, and interest accrual timing is a planner's decision, not a repo tweak. So the loan's *position* is now right while its *payment history* still lists only loan-payment documents |
| **DEF-146** | ⛔ | **THE 2307 WITHHOLDING CERTIFICATE WAS ACCEPTED ON THE INPUT, DOCUMENTED IN THE SCHEMA, AND SILENTLY DROPPED ON SAVE.** `RecordSettlementInput.withholdingCertificateFileId` existed, `channel_settlements.withholding_certificate_file_id` existed with a comment calling it *“the one document that cannot be obtained retroactively”* — and `recordChannelSettlement`'s insert never included it. ⚠️ **An `as never` cast on the insert payload is what hid it**: the repo's `ChannelSettlementRow` had no such field, so TypeScript would have caught the omission and the cast switched the check off. A file attached on screen would have vanished on save, with no error. Found 2026-09-03 reading the insert while adding the preview | ✅ **FIXED, same session** | ✅ The field is on `ChannelSettlementRow`, the cast is gone, and the value is persisted. Closing test: `channel-settlements.test.ts` *“DEF-146 — the 2307 file id is PERSISTED, not silently dropped”*, which reads the column back. ⚠️ **Also found, not fixed:** `FILE_PURPOSES` has no `withholding_certificate` value, so a real upload has to borrow `ar_collection_proof`. Adding one is a CHECK-constraint migration and a naming decision; the column now at least keeps what it is given. ⭐ **The lesson is about the cast, not the field:** an `as never` on a write payload disables exactly the check that would have caught a dropped column, and it leaves no signal at runtime |
| **DEF-147** | ⛔ | ⭐ **`sales_channels.destination_money_account_id` HAD NO WRITER ANYWHERE — SO EVERY REAL CHANNEL WAS UNSETTLEABLE BY CONSTRUCTION.** The column is read on every settlement posting and refused when null (*“there is nowhere to bank this”*), was created by T-150's own migration `0057`, and was set by exactly one thing in the whole repository: a test fixture. `scripts/dev-seed.ts` inserts Foodpanda and GrabFood without it, and **all nine channels in `shareat_dev` read null** — confirmed by query, not by grep. ⚠️ This is why DEF-137 was bigger than a missing screen: the screen would have shipped and refused every settlement anyone tried. Found 2026-09-03 | ✅ **FIXED, same session** | ✅ `setChannelPayoutDestination` (`packages/domain/src/channel-settlements/index.ts`), gated on **`manage_store_settings`** — store-scoped, manager and up. ⭐ **Not `manage_chart_of_accounts`, and the distinction is bible row 149's own:** that rule reserves for an admin the act of *creating a place money can be recorded against*; this creates nothing, it chooses among wallets that already exist, which is routing of the same kind as `receivable_account_code`. Refuses another store's account (*that is a transfer, not a setting* — rows 152–153), refuses a retired one. Surfaced **on the settlement screen** rather than a settings page, because the person who discovers the gap is the person trying to record a payout, and a fix behind a menu they have no reason to open is how a screen stays unusable without anyone reporting it. Three closing tests including a cashier ✗ |
| **DEF-148** | ⚠️ | **`/sales-days/new`'s STORE PICKER DOES NOT RELOAD THE CHANNEL LIST, SO THE FORM LETS YOU BUILD A SUBMISSION THE DOMAIN MUST REFUSE.** The page is a server component with no `onChange` and no client code: the channel rows are rendered from `?store=`, so changing the `<select>` and typing amounts submits **another store's channel ids** and `createSalesDay` refuses with *“channel … is not configured for store … — a day may only report channels its own store has”*. The guard is correct; the form invites the error. Reproduced live on 9008 during the DEF-137 smoketest — it is what made the first two runs fail, and it cost real time to attribute to the app rather than the harness | `verified` — read the page source (no `'use client'`, no handler) and reproduced in a browser | **NOT FIXED — outside DEF-137's scope, and it belongs to whoever owns T-123's screens.** The fix is small and there are two honest shapes: submit-on-change so the page reloads with `?store=`, or drop the picker and reach the form from a store-scoped link. ⚠️ **A third option is worse and should be refused:** rendering every store's channels and filtering client-side would make the mismatch unrepresentable *and* put another store's configuration in the page. Until then, arrive at `/sales-days/new?store=<id>`; a manager scoped to one store never sees it |
| **DEF-150** | ⛔ | ⭐ **`seedCanonicalPositions` (T-154) HAS NO LIVE CALLER ANYWHERE — A FRESH ORG'S HIRE FLOW HAS ZERO POSITIONS TO OFFER, AND `createEmployment` REFUSES A BLANK HIRE OUTRIGHT.** Found 2026-09-04 live-smoketesting T-202/DEF-131's new `/staff/hire` screen: it correctly rendered *"No positions defined for this store yet"* for `shareat_dev`'s own org, and hiring with none checked (the documented "leaves blank → Server" default) would have thrown *"The default position 'Server' is missing for this organisation — the canonical positions have not been seeded."* Grepped: `seedCanonicalPositions` is called from **13 files, every one a test fixture** — not `scripts/dev-seed.ts`, not org creation, not `createPerson`/`createEmployment` themselves. Confirmed at the database level: `select count(*) from positions where org_id = '<dev org>' and store_id is null` returned **0** | `reported` — the six canonical rows were seeded directly into `shareat_dev` by hand (matching `seedCanonicalPositions`'s own SQL exactly) so this task's live smoketest could proceed; the underlying gap — no live code path ever calls it — is untouched | **Out of T-202's scope** (org creation / dev-seed are neither in its file list nor its territory) but blocking for anyone else hiring against a fresh org: `scripts/dev-seed.ts` needs the same call DEF-130's fix added for the super-admin seed, or `createEmployment`/`createPerson` need to lazily seed on first use — a real design decision, not a repo tweak, and named here rather than patched around in a task it does not belong to |
| **DEF-151** | ⛔ | ⭐ **EVERY REAL CALL INTO EITHER FIRESTORE DRIVER FACTORY FAILED WITH "THE FIREBASE SERVICE ACCOUNT DID NOT PARSE AS JSON" — A `??` TREATED A BLANK ENV VAR AS PRESENT.** `loadServiceAccount` (`packages/sync-firestore/src/index.ts`) reads `const raw = inline ?? readKeyFile(file);` where `inline = env.FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY?.trim()`. Nullish-coalescing falls through only on `null`/`undefined` — **not** on `''` — and `.env.local` sets `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY=` to a present-but-blank value (this repo's normal shape: `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY_FILE` is the one actually configured, called out in this same file's own comment as "preferred"). So `raw` was always `''`, and `JSON.parse('')` threw on **every** real invocation of `createFirestoreMirrorDriver`/`createFirestoreTerminalDriver`/`createFirestoreInboxDriver` — silent until now because DEF-132 already established nothing had ever called any of the three outside a test. Found independently and near-simultaneously by T-204 (building `runFirestoreRelay`'s manual verification, `--manual` run against real Firestore: `mirror_publish` failed all 47 pending outbox rows, `inbox_relay` failed outright) and by T-203 (first real caller of a driver factory for its own, unrelated work) | ✅ **FIXED — T-204, 2026-09-04**, same session as the finding | ✅ `const raw = inline || readKeyFile(file);` — `\|\|` treats a blank string the same as absent, matching `file`'s own truthiness check three lines above it. Re-ran the manual verification after the fix: `Shareat · mirror_publish: posted 47, skipped 0, failed 0` / `Shareat · inbox_relay: posted 0, skipped 0, failed 1` (the one deliberately-quarantined throwaway event) — both lanes now genuinely reach real Firestore. `packages/sync-firestore/src/index.ts` was already in T-204's own Scope (it adds `createFirestoreInboxDriver` there), so the fix landed in the same task rather than being logged and left for a separate one. No test previously exercised `loadServiceAccount` against a real (non-empty) file path with this exact env shape — worth a unit test asserting a blank `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` falls through to the file, as a follow-up, not added here to stay inside T-204's own file list |
| **DEF-152** | 🔸 | **`packages/sync-firestore`'s SERVICE-ACCOUNT-FILE READ TRIGGERS A NEXT.JS "TRACE THE WHOLE PROJECT" BUILD WARNING, NOW THAT T-203 GAVE IT ITS FIRST REAL PAGE-LEVEL CALLER.** Found 2026-09-04 running the coordinator's own combined-tree build after T-202/T-203/T-204 all landed. `readKeyFile`'s `join(process.cwd(), path)` (`packages/sync-firestore/src/index.ts:69`) is exactly the pattern Next's static analysis flags as an unbounded filesystem trace — `Server Component: ./packages/sync-firestore/src/index.ts → ./apps/erp/src/app/attendance/terminals/actions.ts → .../page.tsx`. `npm run build` still exits 0; this is a warning, not a failure. ⚠️ **Not a new pattern** — `packages/storage-firebase/src/index.ts:107` has the identical shape (`readFileSync(isAbsolute(path) ? path : join(repoRoot(), path), 'utf8')`) and produces the same warning via `apps/erp/src/instrumentation.ts`'s import chain; that one predates this task entirely and was apparently accepted already, which is why the total warning count (2) matched what T-202 reported before T-203/T-204's page-level Firestore callers existed — the identity of the two warnings shifted, the count coincidentally didn't | `reported` — not investigated further, not fixed | Cosmetic today (build succeeds, deploy has not been measured for the size cost), but worth a real look before the droplet's first deploy: either package's file-read could be marked `/*turbopackIgnore: true*/` (the path is never truly project-relative at runtime — it resolves against `process.cwd()`/`repoRoot()` to a specific secrets file) or scoped to a literal subfolder per Next's own suggested fix. Two independent occurrences of the same shape is worth fixing once, not twice |
| **DEF-153** | ⚠️ | **`PROJECT_BIBLE.md`'s own infra summary table contradicts its own decision log — Cloud SQL / Firebase App Hosting vs. the droplet.** Bible line 598's "Prod hosting" row reads `Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL` with `private IP from Firebase App Hosting`, and `ADR-002-infrastructure-and-auth.md`'s top overview diagram (lines 10-29) and §5 Environments table (lines 206-212, `STAGING`/`PRODUCTION` → Cloud SQL) say the same. But §13 **row 61**, dated the same day (2026-08-22), explicitly states it **"SUPERSEDES the Cloud SQL recommendation"**: the real decision is **Option B — DO Managed Postgres + the app itself on the existing SharEat-Droplet**, both SGP1, same private VPC, because Firebase App Hosting has no static egress IP and a DO-hosted DB would need a public listener or Cloud NAT. ADR-002's own §1 "DECIDED: Option B" section (lines 82-97) says this correctly — only the document's summary/overview sections were never updated to match. | **DEF-170** | ⛔ | **T-157's 57 golden-master divergences diagnosed — 55 of them are a corpus limitation, not an engine bug; v2's formulas were verified byte-for-byte against v1's source.** Read `ERP-rebuild/src/lib/attendance/session-hours.ts` directly against `packages/domain/src/attendance/compute-day.ts`: the night-diff gate (`config.nightDiffEnabled === false ? 0 : …`) and the required-hours cap (`Math.min(rawHours, config.requiredHoursPerShift)`) are **the same formula in both systems**. The divergence traces to `attendanceConfig` being a single live, unversioned document per store (`docs/discovery/01-data-model.md`: "embedded on `stores/{storeId}`, not a subcollection") — v1 never stored a history of it. **The golden capture pairs each historical session's stored result (computed under whatever config was live THEN) with TODAY's config**, so any store where `nightDiffEnabled` or `requiredHoursPerShift` has since changed necessarily "diverges" on replay, through no fault of either engine. Confirmed against the data: every one of the ~39 Lipa night-diff mismatches (v1=2h, v2=0h) has `nightDiffEnabled: false` in its attached (current) config — impossible under v1's own formula unless that flag was true when those August sessions actually ran. Every one of the 16 Malvar/Banay-Banay +1h mismatches (v1=9h, v2=10h) has `requiredHoursPerShift: 10` attached — again only explainable if it was `9` when those sessions ran. **Two residual, smaller items, NOT explained by config drift** (same config both sides, hours match, only the anchor-mode label differs): `lipa-0013` and `banay-0012` — v1 labels the anchor `next`, v2 labels it `exact`, at what looks like a boundary condition in start-anchor resolution; doesn't change the credited hours in either case checked. **One real outlier, unexplained: `lipa-0359`** — v1's engine found no valid anchor at all (`unallowed`) and fell back to a raw-floored start, crediting 9h; v2 resolves a `next` anchor and computes 5h/half-day, a structurally different read of the same punches. Class H fires when night-diff hours alone differ and today's config reads `nightDiffEnabled: false` — **39 specimens**, matching the earlier count exactly. Class I fires when hours alone differ, v2 lands exactly on today's `requiredHoursPerShift`, and there was enough raw presence to have been capped at either value — **15 specimens**, matching exactly. Verified against `HEAD` first (confirmed `ReferenceError` then real, correct failure before the fix), then green for these 55. **The test is currently RED — 3 items remain untriaged and were deliberately NOT forced into a classification:** `lipa-0013` and `banay-0012` (a mode-relabel — `next` vs `exact` — compounding with H/I's own field on the same specimen, not covered by either class's strict single-field signature) and `lipa-0359` (the real, structurally distinct `unallowed`-anchor outlier, root cause not yet found). `npm test` (unit project) will not go green until these 3 are resolved or a further class covers them — flagged here rather than silently left as a surprise for whoever next runs the full suite. ⛔ **SEVERITY RAISED 🔸 → ⛔ on 2026-09-06, and the reason is the important part: this is not a stale test, it is the reason NOTHING HAS EVER BEEN DEPLOYED.** `deploy.yml` is gated on `ci.yml` succeeding (`if: github.event.workflow_run.conclusion == 'success'`), this is CI's **only** failure, and so every `Deploy` run in the repo's history reads **`skipped`** — checked 2026-09-06 via `gh run list`. There is no `/srv/shareat` code and no installed systemd unit on the droplet, because the pipeline that would place them has never once run to completion. ⚠️ **Three consecutive handoffs described this as "known red, unowned, not a regression".** Every word was true and the sentence was badly incomplete: it read as housekeeping when it was a hard production blocker, which is exactly how a red gate becomes furniture. ⛔ **Not triageable by an agent**: `lipa-0359` diverges **v1 = 9 hours vs v2 = 5 hours plus a half-day flag**, so "explaining" it away decides what somebody was paid. That is Ian's call and nobody else's. **✅ FIXED 2026-09-06 — and the engine was not touched: `git diff` on `compute-day.ts` is empty.** Both root causes were in the TRIAGE, not the adjudication. ⭐ **(a) The compound cases.** `lipa-0013` and `banay-0012` each have TWO independently-understood causes, and every class carried a strict single-field signature (`!modeDiffers && !hoursDiffer && …`), so a specimen with two fell through all of them. The distinction that fixes it is real rather than cosmetic: a mode difference where **v1's `shiftStartHour` equals v2's anchor hour** is a relabel with an identical pay basis — a punch at 14:59:32 rounds into the 15:00 start, so v2 says `exact` where v1 said `next` — and it no longer blocks the class explaining the *other* field. `banay-0012` proves its own cause: `cappedAtRequiredHours: true` with today's config reading `requiredHoursPerShift: 10` and v1 storing **9**. ⭐ **(b) `lipa-0359` was the MIRROR OF CLASS D, and class D only ever covered one direction.** v1 recorded `unallowed` with a null anchor; v2 anchored fine, because `anchorWindowMinutes` is a **v2-only setting with no v1 equivalent**. The staffer punched in at **06:40:43** against a grid whose first allowed hour is **07:00** — twenty minutes early on Lipa's stale grid (DEF-087). v1 could not anchor, credited the full 9h cap and flagged `needs_review`; v2 anchors 07:00, credits 8h of real presence, and by **Ian's own `shortShiftHalfDayOt` rule (ON at this store)** books 5h as a half day with 3h raised as PENDING overtime. New **class J**, and it reports into the pay-movement list rather than a silent count. ⚠️ **Class J covers 32 specimens, 33 of the 34 v1-`unallowed` rows being Lipa's** — they were previously absorbed by class C, which asserted *"pay identical"* **without checking**. J checks. ⛔ **Probed, not assumed**: breaking the engine by +30 minutes yields 16 untriaged divergences and shifting every anchor by an hour yields 86, so the relaxed triage still bites and does not swallow a real anchor change. **Unit suite now 1022 passed / 0 failed — green for the first time, which unblocks `ci.yml` and therefore `deploy.yml`.** |

Under normal load the gap between the two is negligible and the floored hour count lands on `30`. Found in passing 2026-09-05 verifying T-209c's gate: two consecutive full `npm run test:integration` runs, each taken during genuine system memory pressure (as low as 1.6 GB free of 15.6 GB, shared across several concurrent Claude Code sessions on this machine — see `project_shareat_system_concurrent_sessions`), each surfaced exactly ONE failure in a DIFFERENT, unrelated file (this one, and separately a widespread-looking 63-failure run that individually re-verified clean — see below); this specific assertion read `29` instead of `30`. Re-run in isolation immediately after (4.5s instead of the ~1000s+ full-suite duration under load): 21/21 pass, including this exact case | `reported` — not independently reproduced on demand; inferred from two data points (fails embedded in a slow/loaded full run, passes instantly isolated) | **Not fixed here** — outside T-209c's scope (reporting), and the fix belongs to whoever owns T-183 (lane health): either inject a fake clock instead of relying on real elapsed wall-clock time between fixture setup and assertion, or widen the assertion to a range (e.g. `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(29)`) so a slow CI run doesn't cross the hour boundary. ⚠️ **Separately, the same investigation surfaced a genuine environmental hazard worth recording even though it produced no defect**: a `test:integration` run started while system-wide free memory was critically low (top consumers were unrelated — browser, multiple Claude Code sessions, a Docker/WSL2 VM, none of them this session's own processes) produced 63 failures spread across 24 completely unrelated files (RLS isolation, journal invariants, stock-locations, production, receipts); re-running two of the individually-affected files in isolation (`journal.test.ts`, `lane-health.test.ts`) immediately after showed both fully green, and a second full run (after free memory recovered from ~1.6 GB to ~3.9 GB) reproduced only this one flake. **The database was never actually corrupted or left inconsistent** — the failures were transient (connection/timeout pressure under memory starvation), not a real regression, and no peer session was running a conflicting `test:integration`/`db:migrate` at the time (confirmed with the peer directly). Worth remembering next time a `test:integration` run looks catastrophically broken on this shared machine: check free memory and re-run before assuming a real regression. |

The real inventory is **ten sites across four files** — seven in `findings.ts` in three mechanisms, plus three more the row never looked for (`channel-settlements.ts:286`, `owners.ts:277`, `deliveries.ts:432`): **(A)** SQL *threshold* on session-tz `current_date` — `supplierCreditAgeing:706`, `customerCreditAgeing:743`, `cashRequestFloatAgeing:848` (fires up to a day early/late on a UTC server); **(B)** displayed *age* recomputed in JavaScript as `(Date.now() − new Date(d+'T00:00:00Z'))/86_400_000` — `serviceChargeFloatAgeing:537`, `reimbursablePayableAgeing:815`, `cashRequestFloatAgeing:853`; **(C)** SQL *age* on `current_date` — the private helper **`storePositions:317`** (feeding `unremittedCeilingBreached` and `remittanceOverdue`). ⚠️ **This correction needed correcting once itself**: a first pass attributed `:317` to `periodicPostingsMissed`, because the scan matched only `export async function` and `storePositions` is private. Same mistake as the original row, made while fixing the original row — recorded because it is the point: **a function name in a defect row is trusted and rarely re-derived.** ⭐ **Mechanism B is the worst and was invisible in the original row**: `cashRequestFloatAgeing` has both A and B, so its gate said *“released over 30 days ago”* on Postgres’s clock while its badge said *“31 days”* on JavaScript’s UTC clock, with nothing making them agree. A date compared to an INSTANT does not cancel the offset; a date compared to a DATE does. | **`fixed`** | **T-218** (`docs/tasks/218-findings-clock-correctness-and-receiving-dispute-detector.md`). All ten converted, and `MANILA_TODAY`/`daysBetweenDates` extracted to a new `packages/db/src/repo/sql-time.ts` so the repo layer has ONE owner for “today”: A and C to `MANILA_TODAY` (`(now() at time zone 'Asia/Manila')::date`, defined ONCE in the module — the seven drifted because every site spelled the date itself); B **structurally**, by computing the age in the same statement that applies the gate (`cashRequestFloatAgeing`) or, where a FIFO walk in TypeScript chooses the date, by carrying `MANILA_TODAY` back as a column and differencing two calendar dates. ⛔ **The JS clock was deleted, not converted** — two clocks that agree today drift tomorrow, which is DEF-178 in another file. ⭐ **The closer is a standing guard, not the edit**: `tests/unit/findings-single-clock.test.ts` fails the build on any bare `current_date` or any `Date.now()` **anywhere in `packages/db/src/repo`** (55 files), with `sql-time.ts` the single exemption. ⭐ **Scoping the guard to the file the defect was noticed in would have declared it closed with three instances still live** — one of them driving an amber badge a person acts on at 14 days. Confirmed failing first with exactly 4 and 3 hits, and re-verified after the fix by reintroducing one of each in real code and watching it bite with correct line numbers. **Lesson: a defect row that names a function is trusted and rarely re-derived — cite the line AND re-read it before filing, because a row that is right about the line and wrong about the function looks verified and sends every future reader to the wrong place.** |

| **DEF-183** | ⚠️ | **`person_roles` is the one access table whose RLS policy does not check that the PERSON belongs to the org — so a role could be assigned to another org's person, and RLS allowed it.** The three access tables are protected two different ways, deliberately and correctly documented in migration `0014`: `person_store_scope` and `person_grants` carry no `org_id`, so their policies derive it *through the person* (`EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM persons p WHERE p.id = ... AND p.org_id = app_current_org())`, with an explicit `WITH CHECK`) — a cross-org write to either is refused by Postgres. `person_roles` carries its **own** `org_id`, so its policy is `org_id = app_current_org() WITH CHECK (org_id = app_current_org())` — **which a row naming THIS org and ANOTHER org's person satisfies perfectly.** The FK to `persons` is satisfied too, because that person genuinely exists. ⭐ **Not reasoned from the policy text — proved**: a test written before any check existed asserted the assignment would be refused and instead reported *"promise resolved instead of rejecting."* Found 2026-09-05 implementing T-219, self-reviewing whether a `personId` arriving from a form needed validating | ✅ **`fixed`** — reproduced by test, closed at the call site by T-219, then **closed in the DATABASE by T-221**; 5 tests guard it | **T-219** — closed at the call site in `assignRoleAsActor` (`packages/auth/src/roles.ts`) with `personsRepo.getPersonById(tx, actor.orgId, personId)`, which returns undefined for a person outside the org. ⚠️ **Severity is limited and worth stating honestly so nobody over-reacts**: the row grants nothing, because `authorize()` resolves a person's role under THEIR org, where it does not exist — and it could never have fired in production anyway, since `assignRole` had no callers (DEF-167). But it is a cross-org write that bible invariant 7 forbids, and a row invisible on both orgs' screens is worse than one that is merely useless. ⭐ **The better fix is a migration** — align `person_roles`'s policy with its two siblings by adding the person-org `EXISTS` clause to its `WITH CHECK`, so the database enforces it rather than one function (RULES §6.3: *"the database enforces the rules, not the service layer"*). Deliberately NOT done in T-219, which changes no schema; recorded in that task's Open questions and **should be picked up by whoever next writes an access migration**. ⭐ **The generalisable half, and the reason this row is written at length**: when sibling tables are protected by two different policy shapes, the shapes are **not interchangeable** — the `org_column` form answers *"is this row labelled with my org?"*, the `via_person` form answers *"does this row's subject belong to my org?"*, and only the second is a real isolation guarantee for a table keyed on a person. Migration `0014` presents all three together, correctly, with a comment explaining why two differ — and a reader comparing them still reasonably concludes they are equivalent, because both end in `= app_current_org()`. ⛔ **The concrete hazard: whoever adds a FOURTH access table will copy whichever policy they happen to open first.** If that is `person_roles`, and the new table is keyed on a person, the hole is re-created silently. **A person-keyed access table MUST use the `via_person` form**, whether or not it also carries an `org_id`. Same shape as DEF-175 — two things that look interchangeable, one of which quietly means something else. ✅ **CLOSED AT THE DATABASE 2026-09-06 by T-221**, migration `0129_def183_person_roles_via_person.sql`: the policy is now `USING (org_id = app_current_org() AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM persons p WHERE p.id = person_roles.person_id AND p.org_id = app_current_org()))`, the identical expression in `WITH CHECK`, and it is **renamed `person_roles_via_person`** so the four person-keyed access policies read as one family. ⭐ **Three things worth carrying forward.** **(1) Both conjuncts, not a swap** — the handoff and this row both said *"add the EXISTS to its WITH CHECK"*, and doing only that would have been wrong: dropping `org_id = app_current_org()` admits the MIRROR row, this org's person given a role in ANOTHER org, which is a privilege grant pointing out of the tenant rather than the dead row described above. The two conjuncts answer independent questions and the test for the mirror (`invariant 2`) **passes before the migration and must keep passing after** — that is what proves the old conjunct was still doing work. **(2) `USING` changed too, not only `WITH CHECK`** — a weaker `USING` leaves an already-written bad row readable and updatable by the org that labelled it, and leaves `UPDATE` able to walk a legitimate row across the boundary; that last one was a **real, separate hole**, found only because a test was written for it (`invariant 2b`, which failed *"the write was ALLOWED"* pre-migration exactly as invariant 1 did). **(3) The ⛔ fourth-table hazard now has an executable guard**, not a comment: `rls.test.ts` *"every person-keyed access table uses the via_person form"* derives the table list from `schema/access.ts` itself and asserts the person-org `EXISTS` appears in **both** `qual` and `with_check`, read live from `pg_policies`. A fourth access table added to that file is covered the day it is added. ⚠️ **Its honest edge**: a person-keyed access table added to a DIFFERENT schema file is caught only by the registry's new `personKeyedAccess` marker, and a marker can be forgotten — this narrows the hazard, it does not eliminate it. The migration also refuses to install over pre-existing violating data (`RAISE EXCEPTION` with the count) rather than hiding it behind a stricter `USING`; **that branch was verified by injecting a violating row on a lane DB inside a rolled-back transaction**, not merely written |
| **DEF-187** | 🔸 | **The RLS registry justified `person_roles` carrying its own `org_id` with a capability that DOES NOT EXIST — *"a person can hold different roles in different orgs, e.g. Ian holding several franchises' admin (bible row 114 A.4)"*.** Found 2026-09-06 while writing T-221, and found *because* the migration would have been WRONG if the sentence were true: the `via_person` clause forbids exactly the row that reason describes. So it was checked rather than trusted, in four places, and it is **unreachable** — `packages/db/schema/sessions.ts` has **no `org_id` column**; `sessionsRepo.findLiveByTokenHash` selects `orgId: persons.orgId`, so a session's org **is** its person's home org by construction; **no org switcher exists** anywhere in `packages/` or `apps/` (the `currentOrgId` in the report pages is a display prop); and `authorize()` axis 3a resolves `activeRoleForPerson(tx, actor.personId, actor.orgId)`. A `person_roles` row whose `org_id` differs from its person's org therefore **has no reader anywhere in the system** — which is exactly why DEF-183's severity was limited, a conclusion that row stated without tracing it to its source | ✅ **`fixed`** — the registry reason is corrected, and the correction names what was checked | **T-221**. `packages/db/src/rls/registry.ts`'s `person_roles` entry now states that the table is org_column **and** person-keyed, that the multi-org sentence was not reachable, what was checked to establish that, and that **migration `0129` must be revisited if bible row 114's franchise-admin story is ever built as one person holding roles across orgs**. ⭐ **The generalisable half, and the reason this is filed rather than quietly edited**: this is the *same shape* as DEF-175/176/180/182 — a claim of coverage with nothing executable behind it — but it wore a different grammatical costume. Those four were catchable by phrases like *"enforced by"* and *"covered by"*; this one is an **"e.g."** — a worked example offered as evidence that a capability exists. ⚠️ It sat in `registry.ts`, the one file whose entire job is to be believed about tenant isolation, and it read as **more** credible than a bare claim would have, because a concrete example feels like proof. **Worth adding "e.g.", "for example", "so that" and "which lets" to DEF-185's sweep vocabulary** — a claim does not have to sound like a guarantee to function as one |
| **DEF-188** | ⛔ | ⭐ **A detector fired on real production-shaped data and every finding it produced was FALSE.** `commissaryUnitMismatch` (T-220, shipped in `ba33acb`) asked *“has this LINE got a `trade_order_receipt` movement?”* and reported two Lipa lines as never received. **Both had their stock.** `confirmReceipt` writes `source_id = orderLineIdByLineId[lineId] ?? orderId`; DEF-140's comment claims *“every real path”* supplies the map, and `shareat_dev` carries **both shapes at once across seven orders**. ⚠️ **Its tests agreed with the bug** because every fixture keyed the movement by line id — the one shape the predicate handled. ⛔ **And it was `hard` severity**, so a false positive did not merely add noise: it blocked period-close readiness for a store whose books were fine. Second defect in the same detector: the detail claimed the amount was *“sitting in 1340 Inventory in transit”* while reporting ₱1,400.00 at the **transfer rate** against a `1340` balance of **₱180.00** — DEF-175's wrong-basis mistake, in a detector written after DEF-175 was filed | **`fixed`** | ⭐ **The opening lesson, because it is the counter-lesson to “verify by running it”: firing on real data proved the query RUNS; it proved nothing about the query being RIGHT. A detector is the one place where a green run and a correct answer look identical.** Found by `ianla-e2` checking the arithmetic behind a number in someone else's already-reviewed, already-tested, already-pushed work — nothing in the author's own process would have caught it. **Fix, and the second attempt matters as much as the first:** the obvious repair (also accept a movement under the order id) has a LIVE blind spot — multi-line orders exist in dev today, so one order-keyed movement would suppress the finding for every line of that order, trading a false positive for a silent miss at `hard` severity on exactly the case the detector exists for. ⭐ **So it now compares QUANTITIES per order** — what the branch signed for across countable lines against what actually moved under *either* `source_id` shape — which is shape-agnostic and keeps multi-line orders detectable. ⛔ **And it reports no peso figure at all, deliberately**: a quantity short and the orders it happened on are what a manager acts on, and a synthetic money number here would be a third basis nobody asked for. Verified on `shareat_dev`: **0 findings**, ground truth `signed_for == into_ledger` on all three received orders including the multi-line one. Two regression tests added — the order-keyed shape, and one pinning the wording so the 1340 claim cannot return. |
| **DEF-189** | 🔸 | **A manager clicking a store in a list they are allowed to read got an HTTP 500, not a refusal.** `/admin/stores` is deliberately ungated — a plain read, matching `/admin/users`' precedent, whose `nav.tsx` comment states the reasoning: *“Not gated per-link … the server refuses them properly. Hiding a link is presentation; the gate is the write.”* ⛔ **On the detail route that claim was false.** `storeReadiness` calls `authorize(…, 'manage_stores')`, which throws, and the throw escaped a Server Component into `app/error.tsx` — a boundary that **deliberately** does not branch on error type, because Next replaces a Server Component error's message with a generic one plus a `digest` in production precisely to stop that detail crossing it. So the manager saw *“Something went wrong”* and a reference code: indistinguishable from a broken page, and it invites a bug report rather than a request for access | **`fixed`** | ⭐ **Found by the T-222 smoketest, and only because it drove the ✗ half as a real browser session rather than asserting the domain function throws.** The integration suite already proved `AuthorizationError` is raised — that test passes and always did. What no test covered was **what the raised error becomes by the time a human reads it**, which is the entire question the nav comment was answering. ⚠️ **The generalisable part, stated precisely because the obvious version of it is wrong.** The trigger is not “an ungated list beside a gated detail page”. It is **calling a DOMAIN function to satisfy a page read**: domain functions authorize by contract, so the moment one is used as a getter, the page inherits a throw the read never intended. `/admin/users` pairs an ungated list with a detail page too and is **clean — probed, HTTP 200 both routes as a manager** — precisely because its reads go straight to the repo and never enter `packages/domain`. ⛔ So the thing to grep for is a page read that calls `packages/domain`, not a missing nav gate. ⚠️ **That grep returns 30+ files in `apps/erp/src/app` and they are NOT swept** — most import pure calculation and are fine, but which of them reach an authorizing domain function is unknown, and finding out is a sweep of its own rather than something T-222 should have quietly absorbed. **Left for Ian to schedule; sized, not done.** **Fix:** `storeDetail` catches `AuthorizationError` **server-side, where `instanceof` is still reliable**, and returns `readiness: null`; the page renders a sentence naming what the viewer may and may not do, with no forms and no checklist. ⛔ Only an authorization denial is converted — anything else keeps throwing, so a broken query cannot hide behind *“you lack access”*. Verified in the browser: manager HTTP 200 with the sentence, 0 forms, no checklist; admin unchanged at 2 forms and a full checklist. |

It was never registered, and **T-173 carries no ‘not built’ entry for it**, unlike its siblings. So v1 stored a silent flag; v2 replaced it with a silently-discarded return value — arguably worse, because v1 at least persisted something | **`fixed`** | Found by the coverage-claims sweep, `docs/discovery/27-coverage-claims-sweep.md` (pass 4). ⭐ **The fix is small and the exposure is not**: the condition is already computed, so this is a registration, not a detector build — raise `commissaryUnitMismatch` from the `trade_order_lines`/`stock_movements` state, or persist the flag `confirmReceipt` already produces. ⚠️ **Until then the operational signature is: stock that never arrives on a balance, value that never leaves `1340`, and no alarm anywhere.** `inventoryControlDoesNotMatchValuation` does not cover it — the ledger and the valuation agree, because the movement was written and the balance was deliberately not. ⛔ Do not ‘fix’ by adding the quantity in the wrong unit; skipping the balance is correct (T-173: *“adding commissary-denominated qty onto a branch balance the branch reads in its own unit is exactly the corruption we're preventing”*). The defect is the silence, not the skip. **✅ FIXED 2026-09-06 by T-220.** `commissaryUnitMismatch` ships in `findings.ts` and is REGISTERED in `findingsFor`. ⭐ **It detects the CONSEQUENCE, not the cause, and that is the design decision worth keeping**: a received internal order line, not `is_ledger_only`, with `received_quantity > 0` and **no `stock_movements` row** (`source_type='trade_order_receipt'`, `source_id` = the line id). The cause — a mismatched base unit — disappears the moment somebody fixes the item, while the goods stay missing from the balance forever; the consequence is persisted and exact. ⚠️ **Correcting this row's own wording**: it said the movement is *“written flagged”*. It is not — `confirmReceipt` `continue`s **before** the write loop, so a mismatched line gets no movement and no layer at all. Skipping is correct and is not what was fixed. ⛔ **`hard` severity**, unlike the ageing detectors around it: goods are physically at the branch, paid for, and absent from its stock ledger, so every valuation and usage figure for that item is wrong until it is fixed. 4 tests, both directions, including a **ledger-only** line (must not fire) and a NULL `received_quantity` (not received is not lost). Verified by unregistering: exactly the positive test fails. ⚠️ **The CAUSE is untouched by design** — persisting the mismatch or wiring `unitMismatches` to a caller is out of T-220's scope. This makes it visible; it does not make it stop happening. |

| **DEF-200** | ⛔ | ⭐ **SEVEN REPORT SCREENS RETURN HTTP 500 BECAUSE TWO ACCESS PREDICATES DISAGREE — `personCoversWholeOrg` says yes and the per-store `authorize()` immediately after says no.** `/reports/ap-aging`, `/ar-aging`, `/cash-flow`, `/income-statement`, `/pos-reconciliation`, `/sales-rollup` and `/store-comparison` all 500 for a **super-admin**, with `AuthorizationError: authorize: person … (role 'super_admin') has no store scope for <storeId>`. ⛔ **The mechanism, in `apps/erp/src/app/reports/lib.ts`:** when `coversWholeOrg` is true the store list becomes **every store in the org** and `storeId` becomes `undefined`; `authorizeForScope` (`lib.ts:145-149`) then proves the capability *per store* by looping `authorize(tx, actor, capability, { storeId })`, and `authorize` checks `accessRepo.personHasStoreScope` — **a row**. So covering the org by ROLE and holding a scope ROW are different facts, and the org-wide branch walks straight into the difference: **one store with no scope row takes out every org-wide report.** ⚠️ **This is bible §13 row 176 / CAPABILITY-MATRIX §C.2 arriving from the other side** — *"a store created LATER appears in nobody's scope"*, and T-222's own report records that a **super_admin creator gets no scope row either**, so the store that triggers it is one the platform operator made. ⚠️ **Second, smaller defect in the same query**: `orgStores` has no `is_active` filter, so a DEACTIVATED store is still listed and still looped over — the store that reproduced this is `ZZ-DEACTIVATED T-222 Smoketest`. Found 2026-09-06 smoketesting T-227, reproduced on all seven routes | `verified` | ⛔ **Not fixed here — outside T-227's scope, and the right fix is a decision rather than a patch.** Three candidates, and they are not equivalent: (1) `authorizeForScope` skips the per-store loop when `coversWholeOrg` (fastest, but it makes org-wide coverage a *second* answer to "may you?", which is how these two predicates came to disagree); (2) `createStore` fans scope rows out to super-admins as well as admins (closes §C.2's remaining half at the source, and is the only one that also fixes every OTHER store-scoped screen); (3) filter deactivated stores out of `orgStores` (correct regardless, and on its own only hides this instance). ✅ **FIXED 2026-09-06, and the root cause is sharper than the three candidates above.** Chasing it to `personCoversWholeOrg` (`repo/access.ts`) showed it computes coverage from **`activeStoreIdsForOrg`** — *active* stores — while `reports/lib.ts`'s own query had **no `is_active` filter at all**. So a person holding every active store was told `coversWholeOrg = true` and then handed a list that also contained the retired ones. ⭐ **Two functions disagreeing about what "the org's stores" means**, which is this project's signature defect shape, and the fix is to make the query match the definition the gate actually consults: one `and(eq(isActive, true))`. ⚠️ **It is not super-admin-only** — verified by signing in as a **manager** as well and getting the identical 500 on all seven. **Verified fixed in a browser**: all ten report routes return 200 for both a super-admin and a manager, and `/reports/ar-aging` shows ₱360.00, tying exactly to the `1210` control balance computed straight from `journal_lines`. ⛔ **Fixed outside T-226's stated Scope and reported as such** (RULES §3.3): a live 500 on seven screens, found while smoketesting, with a one-line fix in the app layer and none in the domain |
| **DEF-201** | ⛔ | ⭐ **A DOUBLE-CLICKED WRITE-OFF BREAKS INVARIANT 6 — the ledger is protected by the idempotency key and the SUBLEDGER IS NOT.** `writeOffCharge` (`packages/domain/src/ar/index.ts:486-455`) posts through `postEntry` with the key `ar_write_off:{chargeId}:{amount}:{personId}`, then calls `arRepo.insertWriteOff` **unconditionally** — it never reads `result.created`. A repeat of the same write-off by the same person for the same amount therefore returns the SAME journal entry and inserts a **SECOND `ar_write_offs` row**. ⛔ **Measured, not reasoned about** — probed against real Postgres 2026-09-06 on a ₱1,000 charge written off ₱400 twice by one admin: `sameEntry: true`, `writeOffRows: 2`, `writeOffTotal: 800.00`, **`1210` control balance `600.00`**, **subledger outstanding `200.00`**. The control account says ₱600 is owed and the aging says ₱200; ₱400 of receivable disappears from the subledger while staying on the control. ⭐ **`voidCharge` and `voidCollection` twenty lines away BOTH guard with `if (result.created)`** — so this is an omission, not a design choice, and its two siblings are the proof. ⚠️ Ian has called duplicate-write-on-multi-click a **fatal** bug class; this is that class reaching the books. Found 2026-09-06 building T-226's write-off form, by reading the idempotency comment and testing whether it was true | `verified` | ⛔ **NOT fixed here.** `docs/tasks/226-*.md` Scope puts `packages/domain/src/ar/index.ts` explicitly out of bounds — *"if one of them turns out to need a change, that is a finding to report, not an edit to make here"* — and this is that case arriving, so the rule is being followed rather than argued with. ⭐ **The fix is one line and it already exists twice in the same file**: wrap `insertWriteOff` in `if (result.created)`, exactly as `voidCharge` wraps `markChargeVoided`. ⚠️ **It wants a test that could not be written before the reads existed**: T-226 added `arRepo.listWriteOffsForCharge`, so a regression test can now assert *rows == entries* for a charge. ⚠️ **And the sibling question the fix must answer**: when the key collides, is the right behaviour to return the first write-off silently (a retry) or to refuse (*"this write-off was already recorded"*)? Silent is right for a double-click and wrong for a deliberate second tranche of the same size on the same day — which the actor component of the key does NOT distinguish |

| **DEF-203** | 🔸 | **`cat >>` onto a file whose last line has NO trailing newline silently glues two settings into one — and it corrupted `APP_TIMEZONE` in production with no error.** Appending `DATABASE_URL=…` to `/srv/shareat-shared/.env.local` during the 2026-09-06 credential rebuild produced one 184-character line reading `APP_TIMEZONE=Asia/ManilaDATABASE_URL=postgresql://…`. ⛔ **Every cheap diagnostic said the file was fine**: `cut -d= -f1` printed `APP_TIMEZONE` (correct — it splits on the FIRST `=`), and `cat -n \| cut -c1-30` hid the tail behind its own truncation. Only `grep -c sslmode` disagreed, returning 2 against a file with one visible URL. **That contradiction was the only tell**, and the right move was to believe the disagreement rather than the majority. ⛔ **Second trap in the same session**: `nl` skips blank lines when numbering, so `nl`'s "line 5" and `grep -n`'s "line 5" were DIFFERENT lines. ⚠️ A pasted leading space is invisible in `cat -n` except as a one-column alignment shift; `tail -1 file \| grep -c ^KEY` settles it. Found 2026-09-06 by `ianla-14` with Ian; recorded 2026-09-07 by the orchestrator session, which held this file | `fixed` | ✅ **Restored on the box the same day** (`APP_TIMEZONE=Asia/Manila`, confirmed against `.env.example:13` and `packages/core/src/env.ts:42`); the live file stayed valid through three failed attempts because of the method that follows. ⭐ **Method worth reusing**: build the file in a STAGING copy under `/root`, verify with `grep -c ^KEY` for every key and a value fragment for each, and only then `cp` over the live file. **Rules**: `printf '\n' >> file` before any `cat >>`; `cat -n`, never `nl`, when cross-referencing with `grep -n`. Written into `docs/ops/README.md` § "Traps on the box" beside DEF-194 and DEF-196 |
| **DEF-204** | ⚠️ | **Constructing a Drizzle handle silently disarms the shared postgres.js client's jsonb and timestamp handling for every other user of that client.** `drizzle-orm/postgres-js`'s `construct()` mutates the client it is given — `client.options.serializers["114"]` and `["3802"]` become an identity function, as do the parsers AND serializers for `1184`/`1082`/`1083`/`1114` (driver.js:17-22). The mutation is on the shared `options` object, so it reaches the pool and every transaction scoped from it, and it is permanent for that client's lifetime. **Consequence: once `getDb()` has been called anywhere in a process, a raw `getSql()` query using `sql.json()` mis-binds** — the array reaches the wire unserialised and Postgres reports *"The 'string' argument must be of type string… Received an instance of Array"* — **and timestamptz columns read back as raw strings rather than `Date`s.** Neither failure is announced and neither is visible in the calling code. ⭐ **It had already bitten someone and was recorded as a symptom with no cause**: `tests/invariants/accounting-equation.test.ts:55` deliberately avoids the shared `getSql()` cache *"which `sql.json()`'s parameter wrapper does not round-trip through cleanly."* Found 2026-09-07 by T-228, where `createOrganization` must seed the chart of accounts inside the same Drizzle transaction as the org row, which is the first caller that ever needed the seeds to run on a Drizzle-constructed client. ⛔ **The trap is that neither obvious binding works in both worlds**: `sql.json(payload)` is correct on a plain client and throws inside a Drizzle transaction; `${json}::jsonb` — and identically `cast(${json} as jsonb)` — is correct inside a Drizzle transaction and throws 22023 *"cannot call jsonb_to_recordset on a non-array"* on a plain client, because postgres.js reads the first adjacent cast as a type hint, tags the parameter jsonb and re-stringifies it. | `fixed` | **Fixed in `packages/db/seed/chart-of-accounts.ts` and `expense-categories.ts`** by binding the payload as text and casting in SQL: `jsonb_to_recordset((${JSON.stringify(payload)})::text::jsonb)`. `::text` tags the parameter as a type neither client rewrites, so the conversion happens in Postgres and the binding is serializer-INDEPENDENT. Verified in all four combinations against real Postgres, and both directions are covered by tests: `packages/db/tests/integration/chart-of-accounts.test.ts` (77 assertions, plain client) and `packages/domain/tests/integration/organizations.test.ts` (inside a Drizzle transaction). ⛔ **Do not "simplify" it back to either single-cast form** — each looks redundant and each breaks exactly one of the two callers. ⚠️ **Blast radius beyond the fix is NOT swept**: any other raw `getSql()` caller relying on `sql.json()` or on `Date`-typed timestamptz reads is subject to the same mutation. Known `sql.json()` sites today are the two seeds (fixed) and `packages/etl/tests/integration/etl-run.test.ts:547,555,580` (unaffected in practice — that suite uses its own client). The timestamp half has no known victim and was not searched for. |

Harmless while the seed is the only writer, live the moment a create path ships — which is exactly why T-229 slice 2 (category creation) must land the normalised index in the same migration. `positions` got this right and says so (`employment.ts:130-135`). Found 2026-09-07 by lane B (ianla-14) specifying T-229 | `reported` | Fix belongs to T-229 slice 2, alongside `createCategory`: the normalised unique index ships in the same migration that first lets a human create a category, never after ⚠️ **The same shape existed on `sales_channels`** — `(org_id, store_id, name)`, case- and whitespace-sensitive — and T-230's migration 0133 replaced it with `lower(trim(name))` in the same migration as that table's first human create path. The rule generalises: every reference table gets the normalised index the day it gains a writer, never after ✅ **CORRECTED 2026-09-07 by T-229 slice 2, migration 0134**: `expense_categories` uniqueness is now `(org_id, lower(trim(category)))`, landed in the same migration as the first human create path; `group` is deliberately not in the key (it would allow "Rent" in OPEX and "rent" in COGS). ⚠️ Found on the way: `seedExpenseCategories`' `on conflict (org_id, category)` named the replaced index and would have failed everywhere — Postgres infers the arbiter from an existing unique index — so the seed now repeats the expression exactly, pinned by a test including idempotency. | **DEF-209** | ⚠️ | ⛔ **The RLS principal machinery has ZERO production callers, so Row-Level Security is NOT the thing enforcing tenant isolation today — the application layer is.** `applyPrincipal`/`withPrincipal` (`packages/db/src/rls/principal.ts:38,69`) are the only way a connection announces which org/actor it is, which is what the RLS policies on every org-scoped table read via `app_current_org()`. Grep across `packages`, `apps`, `scripts`, `tests` (build artifacts excluded) finds them in exactly two source files: their own definition, and `packages/auth/tests/integration/authorize.test.ts`. **Every production script and request path opens a plain `transaction()`**, which connects as the **owner role, for which RLS does not apply** — so `app_current_org()` is never set and the policies never gate a real query. The isolation that actually holds today is `authorize()` plus app-layer `where org_id = …` scoping; RLS is a **dormant safety net**, not the enforcement bible §6.3/§7 describes (*"the database enforces the rules, not the service layer"*). ⚠️ **Consequence:** a single query that forgets its `org_id` predicate leaks across tenants with nothing behind it to catch the mistake — which is the exact class RLS was built to make impossible, and the reason DEF-183/T-221 hardened `person_roles`' policy at all. (2) is there any org-scoped read/write in a production path missing its `org_id` predicate today — i.e. is the dormant net currently load-bearing anywhere? (3) if RLS is to become real, every request path must route through `withPrincipal`, which is a cross-cutting change with its own performance and correctness surface. ⚠️ This does not lower today's security to "broken": app-layer scoping is real and tested. CI needs no change; it inherits the default, and its comment now says the provisioning step is a hard prerequisite rather than preparation for a later flip. ⛔ **Evidence: one clean full-suite run, single invocation, quiet machine, migration 0142 applied — 116 files / 2747 tests / 0 failed (1642s).** The same suite had already been seen green in two halves plus re-runs and that was deliberately judged insufficient: *“it was green in parts”* is a belief, not a measurement. ⭐ **The flip itself was verified three ways with a throwaway probe**, then the probe deleted: unset → `shareat_test_runner`; `=0` → `postgres`; `=1` → `shareat_test_runner`. Green alone would not have shown the default actually moved | ⛔ **A test-side helper cannot fix this — lane D measured it (mutation D)**: revert the job to a bare `transaction()` and all 18 tests still pass, because the job opens its OWN transactions from the pool and a test's `SET LOCAL ROLE` never reaches them. The fix is at the HARNESS: the integration pool must connect as a role that is neither superuser nor `BYPASSRLS` and owns no table (granted membership of `shareat_app` so the existing `asOrg` helpers keep working), so a missing principal fails loudly in every suite — the test-only form of discovery 29's Option B. Until then every new job is probed by hand the way lane D did, and T-216's test 13 proves only that the policy exists and default-denies, which its own comment says |

Not a lockout — the demotion guard still stops the last administrator going — so it is an authority gap, not an alarm. Do not reintroduce it here. T-242 also closed a second door this row never named; see **DEF-237**. Its own task, because the question is what authority removing a role should require, not where to put one line. ⭐ **ANALYSIS, lane D (Opus), 2026-09-08, at the orchestrator's request — the gap is where rows 115 and 116 MEET, and neither alone closes it.** Row 115 already forbids the *effect*: *"nothing anyone DELETES may give anyone else more power… it must never widen access quietly"* — but 115 pictured a deletion widening SOMEBODY ELSE's access (removing an admin auto-promoting every manager), and this is a deletion widening **the actor's own relative authority**: an admin removes the super_admin above them and is unopposed, and since T-232 §2.2 an admin holding the four grants can then appoint admins, so the removal is the *first move of a promotion*. Row 116 supplies the motive it was written to block, in its own words — assigning an admin stays with super_admin *"because it stops an admin minting a peer admin, a lateral move invisible from above"* — and removing the super_admin first reaches the same end through a door 116 never mentions. **The code is the only place the two rows are joined**, so a task that adds a check without a row leaves the next person deriving this gap a third time. **Proposed rule, ⛔ AWAITING IAN (see below): the authority to UNMAKE a role is the authority to MAKE it — one rule, both directions.** Concretely an actor may not remove or demote a role **senior** to their own; a **peer** may. ⚠️ Peer-removal rather than junior-only is deliberate: "you may only unmake roles junior to your own" would leave a *departed* super_admin removable by nobody but Ian, and row 116 already records *"two actions now depend on Ian personally"* as known and accepted — this must not make it three by accident. ⛔ **The case that has to be decided out loud, because it will be raised**: one super_admin who has left and one admin remaining — under peer-removal that admin still cannot strip them, and the answer has to be *"that is Ian's, like appointment is"*, or the rule is not a rule. **This is Ian's call and is on his board.** ⚠️ **Urgency, stated so nobody misreads it: an authority gap, not a safety one.** T-238's lockout guard already stops the last administrator who can sign in from going, so no removal can empty an organisation — what is missing is the *ordering*, not the floor; and production holds one person, so exposure today is zero. The row is worth writing because this code is about to get more callers, not because anything is bleeding. ⚠️ Attach the rule to the **gated entry point** (`assignRoleAsActor`), not the primitive: `accessRepo.revokeActiveRole` is deliberately ungated exactly as `assignRole` is, and any future "remove this person's role entirely" path must inherit the row rather than re-derive it — the DEF-180 shape row 116 was itself written after |

| **DEF-237** | ⚠️ | ⭐ **A manager elevated because the organisation had no administrator keeps those powers for ever — nothing revokes them when an administrator arrives.** `assignRole` (`packages/auth/src/roles.ts:96-108`) applies the whole of `ELEVATED_GRANTS_FOR_ADMINLESS_MANAGER` — derived at `capabilities.ts:777` as exactly the admin-yes/manager-no set, which **includes `manage_users`** (`capabilities.ts:688`) — to any manager appointed while `hasActiveAdmin` is false.
